# Sources Register

Short, durable source register for this revision. Prefer primary or institutional sources.

## Energy / compute

- **[S01] IEA — Energy demand from AI**  
  Why it matters: projects data-centre electricity demand and identifies local grid bottlenecks.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/reports/energy-and-ai/energy-demand-from-ai

- **[S02] IEA — Electricity 2025**  
  Why it matters: frames the present as an “Age of Electricity” driven by electrification, cooling, and data centres.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/reports/electricity-2025

- **[S03] IRENA — Record-Breaking Annual Growth in Renewable Power Capacity**  
  Why it matters: shows 2024 renewable-capacity expansion and its scale relative to total power expansion.  
  URL: https://www.irena.org/News/pressreleases/2025/Mar/Record-Breaking-Annual-Growth-in-Renewable-Power-Capacity

## Climate / water / migration

- **[S04] WMO — State of Global Water Resources 2024**  
  Why it matters: summarizes drought, flood, glacier-loss, and basin-normality signals relevant to settlement pressure.  
  URL: https://wmo.int/resources/publication-series/state-of-global-water-resources/state-of-global-water-resources-2024

- **[S05] NOAA — 2024 was the world’s warmest year on record**  
  Why it matters: establishes recent thermal context.  
  URL: https://www.noaa.gov/news/2024-was-worlds-warmest-year-on-record

- **[S06] NOAA / NCEI — U.S. Billion-Dollar Weather and Climate Disasters**  
  Why it matters: shows recent disaster frequency and costs as adaptation pressure.  
  URL: https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/access/billions/

- **[S07] World Bank — Groundswell report press release**  
  Why it matters: gives a high-level estimate of potential internal climate migration and the role of mitigation/adaptation.  
  URL: https://www.worldbank.org/en/news/press-release/2021/09/13/climate-change-could-force-216-million-people-to-migrate-within-their-own-countries-by-2050

## Biology / sensing / public health

- **[S08] NHGRI — DNA Sequencing Costs: Data**  
  Why it matters: documents the long-run cost collapse in sequencing.  
  URL: https://www.genome.gov/about-genomics/fact-sheets/DNA-Sequencing-Costs-Data

- **[S09] CDC — About CDC’s National Wastewater Surveillance System**  
  Why it matters: shows wastewater monitoring as early, national-scale, population-level biosensing.  
  URL: https://www.cdc.gov/nwss/about.html

- **[S10] WHO — Global genomic surveillance strategy for pathogens with pandemic and epidemic potential 2022–2032**  
  Why it matters: formalizes genomic surveillance as durable public-health infrastructure.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/initiatives/genomic-surveillance-strategy

- **[S11] WHO — Six years after COVID-19’s global alarm**  
  Why it matters: gives a recent implementation signal, including strengthened genomic surveillance in 110+ countries.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/news/item/02-02-2026-six-years-after-covid-19-s-global-alarm-is-the-world-better-prepared-for-the-next-pandemic

- **[S12] NIH Common Fund — HuBMAP**  
  Why it matters: frames cellular-resolution human mapping as an open platform and atlas-building project.  
  URL: https://commonfund.nih.gov/HuBMAP

## Demography / ageing

- **[S13] WHO — Ageing and health**  
  Why it matters: concise current demographic ageing benchmarks.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/ageing-and-health

- **[S14] United Nations — Population / World Population Prospects 2024 materials**  
  Why it matters: gives current fertility decline and below-replacement context.  
  URL: https://www.un.org/en/global-issues/population

## Orbit / congestion / governance

- **[S15] ESA — Space Environment Report 2025**  
  Why it matters: establishes current debris growth, object counts, and the case for active debris removal.  
  URL: https://www.esa.int/Space_Safety/Space_Debris/ESA_Space_Environment_Report_2025

- **[S16] FAA — Commercial Space Transportation**  
  Why it matters: indicates growth in licensed commercial operations and the rising administrative reality of routine launches.  
  URL: https://www.faa.gov/space

## Provenance / trust / credentials

- **[S17] C2PA — Verifying Media Content Sources**  
  Why it matters: describes Content Credentials as an open provenance and authenticity standard.  
  URL: https://c2pa.org/

- **[S18] C2PA — Technical Specification**  
  Why it matters: defines signed assertions, claims, manifests, and provenance structure for digital content.  
  URL: https://spec.c2pa.org/specifications/specifications/2.3/specs/C2PA_Specification.html

- **[S19] European Commission — Navigating the AI Act**  
  Why it matters: states the Article 50 transparency obligations and their application date.  
  URL: https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/faqs/navigating-ai-act

- **[S20] European Commission — First draft Code of Practice on marking and labelling of AI-generated content**  
  Why it matters: shows implementation work around machine-readable marking and disclosure rules ahead of August 2026 applicability.  
  URL: https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/commission-publishes-first-draft-code-practice-marking-and-labelling-ai-generated-content

- **[S21] W3C — Verifiable Credentials Data Model v2.0**  
  Why it matters: formalizes tamper-evident digital claims and selective disclosure in issuer-holder-verifier ecosystems.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-data-model-2.0/

- **[S22] W3C — Verifiable Credentials 2.0 press release**  
  Why it matters: provides the current standards status and concise explanation of the trust model.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/press-releases/2025/verifiable-credentials-2-0/

- **[S23] NIST — Generative AI Profile**  
  Why it matters: explicitly treats provenance tracking, watermarking, and transparency mechanisms as risk-management tools.  
  URL: https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/NIST.AI.600-1.pdf

## Insurance / underwriting / housing

- **[S24] Swiss Re Institute — sigma 1/2025: Natural catastrophes: insured losses on trend to USD 145 billion in 2025**  
  Why it matters: establishes the scale and trend of insured catastrophe losses and the protection gap.  
  URL: https://www.swissre.com/institute/research/sigma-research/sigma-2025-01-natural-catastrophes-trend.html

- **[S25] Munich Re — Natural disaster figures 2025**  
  Why it matters: gives recent insured-loss totals and confirms repeated years above USD 100 billion.  
  URL: https://www.munichre.com/en/company/media-relations/media-information-and-corporate-news/media-information/2026/natural-disaster-figures-2025.html

- **[S26] FHFA — Natural Disaster Risk**  
  Why it matters: shows that housing-finance regulators are operationalizing insurance and disaster risk through symposia, dashboards, and enterprise oversight.  
  URL: https://www.fhfa.gov/programs/natural-disaster-risk

- **[S27] Freddie Mac — The Cost of Homeowners’ Insurance**  
  Why it matters: shows heterogeneous insurance burdens across borrowers and geographies, including stronger effects on lower-income households.  
  URL: https://www.freddiemac.com/research/insight/homeowners-insurance-costs

- **[S28] Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis — Rising property insurance costs stress multifamily housing**  
  Why it matters: documents rent pass-through, shrinking coverage, and development pressure in multifamily housing.  
  URL: https://www.minneapolisfed.org/article/2025/rising-property-insurance-costs-stress-multifamily-housing

- **[S29] Federal Reserve Board — Speech by Vice Chair for Supervision Barr on supervision and regulation**  
  Why it matters: explicitly links insurance-market pricing to development, adaptation, access to financial services, and financial stability.  
  URL: https://www.federalreserve.gov/newsevents/speech/barr20250220a.htm

- **[S30] NAIC — Natural Catastrophe Risk Dashboard Report**  
  Why it matters: provides regulator-facing summary signals on rate increases, non-renewals, residual markets, and catastrophe-related insurance stress.  
  URL: https://content.naic.org/sites/default/files/call_materials/Natural%20Catastrophe%20Risk%20Dashboard%20Report%202.20.26.pdf

- **[S31] FEMA — Understanding Flood Risk: Real Estate, Lending and Insurance Professionals**  
  Why it matters: reflects the direct linkage between hazard designation, lender requirements, and mandatory flood insurance in parts of the mortgage market.  
  URL: https://www.fema.gov/flood-maps/know-your-risk/realtor-lending-insurance

## Permitting / clearance / administrative capacity

- **[S32] IEA — IEA Support to Accelerating Renewable Energy Permitting (ARPE)**  
  Why it matters: shows renewable deployment being treated as an administrative-streamlining and public-engagement problem, not just a technology problem.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/reports/iea-support-to-accelerating-renewable-energy-permitting-arpe

- **[S33] European Commission — Renewables Directive sectoral targets reach transposition deadline**  
  Why it matters: states that full implementation of the accelerated-permitting provisions is decisive for the required increase in renewable generation.  
  URL: https://energy.ec.europa.eu/news/renewables-directive-sectoral-targets-reach-transposition-deadline-2025-05-21_en

- **[S34] European Commission — Renewable Energy Directive**  
  Why it matters: shows the revised directive’s earlier permitting-transposition deadlines and the broader policy effort to accelerate renewable deployment.  
  URL: https://energy.ec.europa.eu/topics/renewable-energy/renewable-energy-directive-targets-and-rules/renewable-energy-directive_en

- **[S35] IEA — Electricity Grids and Secure Energy Transitions**  
  Why it matters: shows that grid infrastructure often takes five to fifteen years to plan, permit, and complete.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/reports/electricity-grids-and-secure-energy-transitions

- **[S36] U.S. Department of Energy — Coordinated Interagency Transmission Authorizations and Permits Program**  
  Why it matters: shows a current federal attempt to compress transmission permitting into a two-year timeline and treat process time as a strategic barrier.  
  URL: https://www.energy.gov/oe/coordinated-interagency-transmission-authorizations-and-permits-program

- **[S37] World Bank — Business Ready 2025**  
  Why it matters: generalizes the point that delays in permits and public-service transactions raise costs, create uncertainty, and discourage investment.  
  URL: https://ppp.worldbank.org/sites/default/files/2026-01/Full%20Book.pdf

- **[S38] OECD — Economic Surveys: Canada 2025**  
  Why it matters: ties housing affordability and supply to approval times, cumbersome municipal permitting, and the use of electronic or one-stop systems.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/content/dam/oecd/en/publications/reports/2025/05/oecd-economic-surveys-canada-2025_ee18a269/28f9e02c-en.pdf

- **[S39] OECD — Economic Surveys: Netherlands 2025**  
  Why it matters: links housing shortages to overlapping planning processes, long appeal procedures, and related regulatory constraints.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/oecd-economic-surveys-netherlands-2025_2dd1f4aa-en.html

- **[S40] World Bank — Peru Project Appraisal Document (March 2026)**  
  Why it matters: extends the clearance thesis into mining and industrial development by linking permitting delays and overlapping mandates to lost production and investment.  
  URL: https://documents.worldbank.org/curated/en/099022426150017420/pdf/BOSIB-f7cd0b19-acea-491c-97eb-b608e74ea01f.pdf


## Cleanup / decommissioning / end-of-life governance

- **[S41] IEA — Recycling of Critical Minerals**  
  Why it matters: frames recycling as indispensable to secure and sustainable critical-mineral supply and as a way to reduce landfilling of end-use technologies.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/reports/recycling-of-critical-minerals

- **[S42] European Commission — Batteries**  
  Why it matters: shows the EU treating batteries as life-cycle-governed products, from sourcing to collection, recycling, and repurposing.  
  URL: https://environment.ec.europa.eu/topics/waste-and-recycling/batteries_en

- **[S43] European Commission — New rules to boost recycling efficiency from waste batteries (4 July 2025)**  
  Why it matters: sets concrete recycling-efficiency and material-recovery targets, showing end-of-life processing becoming a governed industrial layer.  
  URL: https://environment.ec.europa.eu/news/new-rules-boost-recycling-efficiency-waste-batteries-2025-07-04_en

- **[S44] European Commission Joint Research Centre — There’s new waste coming from the transition to renewables. How to reuse and recycle it?**  
  Why it matters: quantifies the coming material waste streams from PV, wind, and fossil-plant decommissioning and argues for secondary markets to be built in parallel with the transition.  
  URL: https://joint-research-centre.ec.europa.eu/jrc-news-and-updates/theres-new-waste-coming-transition-renewables-how-reuse-and-recycle-it-2025-03-11_en

- **[S45] IRENA — Solar energy**  
  Why it matters: establishes the scale and pace of PV deployment that will eventually generate a correspondingly large end-of-life management problem.  
  URL: https://www.irena.org/Energy-Transition/Technology/Solar-energy

- **[S46] ITU — The Global E-waste Monitor 2024**  
  Why it matters: provides current official context on global e-waste growth, uneven formal collection, and the policy importance of extended producer responsibility.  
  URL: https://www.itu.int/en/ITU-D/Environment/Pages/Publications/The-Global-E-waste-Monitor-2024.aspx

- **[S47] U.S. EPA — Interim Guidance on the Destruction and Disposal of PFAS and Materials Containing PFAS**  
  Why it matters: treats destruction, disposal, and release control for PFAS as an ongoing governance and technology problem rather than a closed technical issue.  
  URL: https://www.epa.gov/pfas/interim-guidance-destruction-and-disposal-pfas-and-materials-containing-pfas

- **[S48] U.S. EPA — Key EPA Actions to Address PFAS**  
  Why it matters: ties cleanup, disposal, reporting, drinking-water standards, and Superfund liability into a single regulatory tail.  
  URL: https://www.epa.gov/pfas/key-epa-actions-address-pfas

- **[S49] ESA — Space Environment Report 2025**  
  Why it matters: says existing debris alone is enough to risk runaway collisions and shows active debris removal becoming necessary.  
  URL: https://www.esa.int/Space_Safety/Space_Debris/ESA_Space_Environment_Report_2025

- **[S50] ESA — ESA’s Zero Debris approach**  
  Why it matters: shows a leading space agency treating guaranteed disposal and active removal as design requirements for future missions.  
  URL: https://www.esa.int/Space_Safety/Clean_Space/ESA_s_Zero_Debris_approach

- **[S51] BOEM — Decommissioning White Paper**  
  Why it matters: shows offshore wind being governed from the start by conceptual decommissioning plans and financial assurance sufficient to cover removal costs.  
  URL: https://www.boem.gov/sites/default/files/documents/renewable-energy/state-activities/Decommissioning%20White%20Paper.pdf


## Legibility / registries / standards

- **[S52] World Bank — World Development Report 2025: Standards for Development**  
  Why it matters: treats standards as hidden economic infrastructure and ties them directly to trade, services, and quality infrastructure.  
  URL: https://www.worldbank.org/en/publication/wdr2025

- **[S53] World Bank ID4D — Home**  
  Why it matters: provides current top-line estimates on the population still lacking official ID and digital ID for official online transactions.

- **[S54] World Bank ID4D — Global Dataset**  
  Why it matters: gives current country-count and population signals on government-recognized digital identity for online authentication.  
  URL: https://id4d.worldbank.org/global-dataset

- **[S55] World Bank — Colombia’s Multipurpose Cadaster**  
  Why it matters: shows updated cadastral information being treated as a tool for land rights, planning, taxation, infrastructure, credit, and investment climate.  
  URL: https://www.worldbank.org/en/news/press-release/2025/11/18/el-banco-mundial-apoya-la-ampliacion-del-catastro-multiproposito-en-colombia

- **[S56] W3C — Verifiable Credentials Data Model v2.0**  
  Why it matters: formalizes tamper-evident digital claims in issuer-holder-verifier ecosystems.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-data-model-2.0/

- **[S57] European Commission — Commission launches consultation on the Digital Product Passport**  
  Why it matters: shows product-level machine-readable data becoming a formal governance and enforcement layer under the ESPR.

- **[S58] GLEIF — The LEI in Numbers: Global Transparency and Digitalization Push Drives LEI Adoption in 2025**  
  Why it matters: shows legal-entity identity becoming more widespread and more tightly linked to regulatory and cross-border interoperability demands.

- **[S59] World Bank — Digital Public Infrastructure and Services**  
  Why it matters: frames digital ID, payments, and data exchange as reusable public rails for cross-sector service delivery.  
  URL: https://www.worldbank.org/ext/en/topic/digital-and-ai/digital-public-infrastructure-and-services


## Dual-use infrastructure / strategic backbone

- **[S60] NATO — Deterrence and defence**  
  Why it matters: shows critical infrastructure and network protection entering alliance-level defence spending and resilience planning rather than remaining a purely civilian concern.  
  URL: https://www.nato.int/en/what-we-do/deterrence-and-defence/deterrence-and-defence

- **[S61] European Commission — Critical infrastructure resilience at EU-level**  
  Why it matters: shows the CER framework treating essential services across eleven sectors — including digital infrastructure and space — as resilience and security obligations.  
  URL: https://home-affairs.ec.europa.eu/policies/internal-security/counter-terrorism-and-radicalisation/protection/critical-infrastructure-resilience-eu-level_en

- **[S62] European Commission — Joint Communication to strengthen the security and resilience of submarine cables**  
  Why it matters: treats both submarine communication cables and electricity cables as strategic infrastructure requiring prevention, detection, response, repair, and deterrence.  
  URL: https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/factpages/joint-communication-strengthen-security-and-resilience-submarine-cables

- **[S63] NATO — NATO strengthens cooperation with industry to protect critical undersea infrastructure**  
  Why it matters: shows civilian authorities, militaries, and private operators being pulled into shared protection of cables and pipelines that underpin connectivity and energy security.  
  URL: https://www.nato.int/en/news-and-events/articles/news/2025/05/26/nato-strengthens-cooperation-with-industry-to-protect-critical-undersea-infrastructure

- **[S64] NATO — NATO Commercial Space Strategy**  
  Why it matters: formalizes alliance-level integration of commercial space solutions into training, exercises, operations, and interoperability work.  
  URL: https://www.nato.int/en/about-us/official-texts-and-resources/official-texts/2025/02/13/nato-commercial-space-strategy

- **[S65] U.S. Space Force — Commercial Space Strategy**  
  Why it matters: shows a major military service explicitly building hybrid architectures around commercial launch, satellite communications, sensing, and navigation services.  
  URL: https://www.spaceforce.mil/Portals/2/Documents/Space%20Policy/USSF_Commercial_Space_Strategy.pdf

- **[S66] U.S. Department of the Treasury — The Committee on Foreign Investment in the United States (CFIUS)**  
  Why it matters: shows ownership, real-estate proximity, and foreign investment in strategic assets being treated as national-security variables.  
  URL: https://home.treasury.gov/policy-issues/international/the-committee-on-foreign-investment-in-the-united-states-cfius

- **[S67] European Commission — Investment screening**  
  Why it matters: shows foreign-investment review being generalized as a tool for protecting security and public order across strategically sensitive sectors and projects.  
  URL: https://policy.trade.ec.europa.eu/enforcement-and-protection/investment-screening_en

- **[S68] CISA — Critical Infrastructure Sectors / Risk Management**  
  Why it matters: shows U.S. infrastructure governance treating sixteen sectors — and even space-system resilience — as part of a common critical-infrastructure risk framework.  
  URL: https://www.cisa.gov/topics/critical-infrastructure-security-and-resilience/critical-infrastructure-sectors


## Preparedness / warning / continuity

- **[S69] WHO — WHO Pandemic Agreement**  
  Why it matters: shows Member States formalizing pandemic prevention, preparedness, and response as standing international governance rather than one-off crisis improvisation.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/health-topics/who-pandemic-agreement

- **[S70] WHO — International health regulations (2005)**  
  Why it matters: states that the amended IHR text is current since 19 September 2025 for most States Parties, showing preparedness and reporting obligations being updated post-COVID.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/health-topics/international-health-regulations

- **[S71] WHO — Global health emergency corps framework**  
  Why it matters: formalizes workforce, coordination, and surge structures for health emergencies as durable institutional capacity.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/9789240109445

- **[S72] WHO — WHO brings countries together to test collective pandemic response**  
  Why it matters: shows simulation exercises and cross-border operational testing becoming part of routine preparedness infrastructure.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/news/item/04-04-2025-who-brings-countries-together-to-test-collective-pandemic-response

- **[S73] WMO — Early Warnings for All**  
  Why it matters: frames universal multi-hazard early warning coverage as a global policy target by 2027.  
  URL: https://wmo.int/activities/early-warnings-all

- **[S74] WMO / UNDRR — Global Status of Multi-Hazard Early Warning Systems 2025**  
  Why it matters: gives current adoption levels and remaining gaps, showing warning coverage turning into a measurable governance benchmark.  
  URL: https://wmo.int/publication-series/global-status-of-multi-hazard-early-warning-systems-2025

- **[S75] FEMA — Integrated Public Alert & Warning System (IPAWS)**  
  Why it matters: shows authenticated public alerting as national standing infrastructure rather than an ad hoc communication channel.  
  URL: https://www.fema.gov/emergency-managers/practitioners/integrated-public-alert-warning-system

- **[S76] CDC — About CDC’s National Wastewater Surveillance System**  
  Why it matters: shows continuous biosurveillance becoming part of ordinary public-health preparedness.  
  URL: https://www.cdc.gov/nwss/about.html

- **[S77] European Commission — EU Preparedness Union Strategy to prevent and react to emerging threats and crises**  
  Why it matters: explicitly calls for a preparedness-by-design culture, minimum preparedness criteria for essential services, 72-hour household readiness, and stronger whole-of-society coordination.  
  URL: https://civil-protection-humanitarian-aid.ec.europa.eu/news-stories/news/eu-preparedness-union-strategy-prevent-and-react-emerging-threats-and-crises-2025-03-26_en

- **[S78] European Commission — Stockpiling**  
  Why it matters: shows the EU shifting from reactive crisis management toward integrated stockpiling of essential goods, energy equipment, raw materials, and water-related supplies.  
  URL: https://civil-protection-humanitarian-aid.ec.europa.eu/what/civil-protection/stockpiling_en

- **[S79] European Commission — Medical Countermeasures Strategy**  
  Why it matters: treats development, production, stockpiling, and rapid deployment of critical medical tools as standing all-hazards preparedness infrastructure.  
  URL: https://health.ec.europa.eu/health-emergency-preparedness-and-response-hera/preparedness/medical-countermeasures-strategy_en

- **[S80] FEMA — Continuity Resource Toolkit**  
  Why it matters: shows uninterrupted essential functions being governed through formal continuity planning rather than left to improvisation.  
  URL: https://www.fema.gov/emergency-managers/national-preparedness/continuity


## Reliability / maintenance / field execution

- **[S81] IEA — World Energy Employment 2025 (Executive summary)**  
  Why it matters: says skilled-worker pipelines are now strategic for energy security and that labour shortages are already affecting timelines, reliability, and cost control.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/reports/world-energy-employment-2025/executive-summary

- **[S82] IEA — Building the Future Transmission Grid (Executive summary)**  
  Why it matters: shows that skilled labour is needed across the full chain of transmission development, including maintenance.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/reports/building-the-future-transmission-grid/executive-summary

- **[S83] U.S. EPA — Water Infrastructure Sector Workforce**  
  Why it matters: shows that utilities face recruitment and retention pressure, that roughly one-third of the workforce is eligible to retire in the next decade, and that more specialized technical skills are needed.  
  URL: https://www.epa.gov/sustainable-water-infrastructure/water-infrastructure-sector-workforce

- **[S84] U.S. EPA — EPA’s 7th Drinking Water Infrastructure Needs Survey and Assessment**  
  Why it matters: estimates USD 625 billion in 20-year drinking-water infrastructure needs, reinforcing the upkeep burden behind essential services.  
  URL: https://www.epa.gov/dwsrf/epas-7th-drinking-water-infrastructure-needs-survey-and-assessment

- **[S85] FAA — Facility Replacement and Radar Modernization**  
  Why it matters: shows national transport reliability depending on modernization of aging air-traffic-control facilities and radars.  
  URL: https://www.faa.gov/frrm

- **[S86] WHO — Decent work for health and care workers**  
  Why it matters: states the projected global deficit of 11 million health workers by 2030 and frames care workers as vital to resilient health systems.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/teams/health-workforce/decentwork

- **[S87] WHO — State of the world’s nursing report 2025**  
  Why it matters: treats nurses as an indispensable occupational group and calls for country-level investment in stronger health systems.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/9789240110236

- **[S88] ASCE — 5 key takeaways from the 2025 Report Card for America’s Infrastructure**  
  Why it matters: summarizes the persistence of deferred-maintenance pressure and the scale of investment needed to reach a state of good repair.  
  URL: https://www.asce.org/publications-and-news/civil-engineering-source/article/2025/03/27/5-key-takeaways-from-the-2025-report-card-for-americas-infrastructure


## Scientific platforms / continuous observation

- **[S89] NASA — Earth System Observatory**  
  Why it matters: shows Earth science being organized around a coordinated mission stack intended to produce a holistic, three-dimensional view of Earth.  
  URL: https://science.nasa.gov/earth-science/missions/earth-system-observatory/

- **[S90] NASA — Earth System Science Research**  
  Why it matters: states that NASA Earth science advances understanding by integrating satellite, airborne, surface, and partner datasets with predictive models and data-analysis tools.  
  URL: https://science.nasa.gov/earth-science/research/

- **[S91] Copernicus Data Space Ecosystem — Home**  
  Why it matters: shows Europe treating Earth-observation data and services as a shared, continuously accessible platform rather than as isolated mission outputs.  
  URL: https://dataspace.copernicus.eu/

- **[S92] NOAA — U.S. Integrated Ocean Observing System (IOOS)**  
  Why it matters: frames ocean science as an integrated network of people and technology that gathers observing data and develops predictive tools for decision use.  
  URL: https://ioos.noaa.gov/

- **[S93] NIH All of Us — Largest data expansion to date**  
  Why it matters: shows health research shifting toward maintained platforms with large linked datasets, genomics, and wearable-device streams.

- **[S94] NSF — Programmable Cloud Laboratories initiative**  
  Why it matters: shows laboratory science moving toward remotely accessible, AI-enabled workflows and shared programmable experimental infrastructure.  
  URL: https://www.nsf.gov/news/nsf-invest-new-national-network-ai-programmable-cloud

- **[S95] NSF — Integrated Data Systems & Services (IDSS)**

## Epistemic inequality / feedback advantage

- **[S97] ITU — Facts and Figures 2025**  
  Why it matters: shows that 2.2 billion people remain offline in 2025 and that quality, affordability, and urban-rural connectivity gaps persist.

- **[S98] World Bank — Digital Progress and Trends Report 2025: Strengthening AI Foundations**

- **[S99] OECD — Governing with Artificial Intelligence**  
  Why it matters: says AI can improve government productivity and responsiveness but that government adoption lags because of skills shortages, legacy systems, data constraints, and fiscal pressure.

  Why it matters: provides a concrete indicator of compute concentration and the practical inequality of AI capability.

## Education bifurcation / acceleration vs stabilization

- **[S102] OECD — OECD Digital Education Outlook 2026**

- **[S105] UNESCO — AI competency framework for teachers**

- **[S106] UNICEF — Foundations First: Insights from the Global Foundational Learning Action Tracker 2025**

- **[S108] UNESCO — What you need to know about education for health and well-being**

  Why it matters: treats health and well-being as a core component of transformative education and a planning variable for ministries.

- **[S110] World Bank — Education & Skills / Building Resilient Schools (RIGHT+ Framework)**  
  Why it matters: shows resilient, inclusive, healthy, and learning-conducive school environments being treated as system design requirements.

- **[S111] OECD — The OECD Learning Compass 2030**  
  Why it matters: makes individual and collective well-being a formal orienting goal of future education rather than a peripheral add-on.

## Family policy as macroeconomic infrastructure

- **[S112] OECD — Child and family well-being**

- **[S113] OECD — Women, work and the population puzzle**

- **[S114] OECD — Fertility, employment and family policy**

- **[S115] OECD — Employment Outlook 2025: Demographic change, economic growth and intergenerational inequalities**

- **[S117] ILO — Advancing decent work and the care economy**

- **[S118] UNFPA — The real fertility crisis / press release and report page**  
  Why it matters: reframes fertility stress around constrained reproductive agency, financial limits, housing, job insecurity, unequal care burdens, and fear of the future.

- **[S119] World Bank — Invest in Childcare**  
  Why it matters: treats childcare explicitly as a human-capital, equality, business, and economic-growth investment with operational country programs.

- **[S120] World Bank — Five ways evidence is unlocking women’s economic participation**  
  Why it matters: shows recent country-level policy translation, including care-economy roadmaps, childcare standards, and labour-force targets.

- **[S121] World Bank — Women, Business and the Law 2026 / flagship overview**

- **[S122] World Bank — Toward Available, Affordable, and Quality Childcare**  
  Why it matters: provides a compact institutional framing of childcare as availability, affordability, quality, and implementation capacity, not just household preference.

## Competent second-tier cities / settlement sorting

- **[S123] OECD — Programme: Unlocking the potential of intermediary cities**  
  Why it matters: states that intermediary cities can alleviate excessive housing costs and congestion, offer accessible services and quality of life, and attract people and firms as part of balanced territorial development.

- **[S124] OECD/UN-Habitat — Intermediary Cities and Climate Change**

- **[S126] World Bank — Cities as Growth Hubs: Knowledge Framework**

- **[S127] OECD — Urban housing**

- **[S128] World Bank — New Project Boosts Infrastructure and Land Services in Cameroon**  
  Why it matters: shows direct investment in climate-resilient urban infrastructure and land administration as a strategy to unlock the potential of targeted urban areas.

- **[S129] World Bank — New Project Improves Urban Infrastructure and Reduces Flood Risk in Gabon’s Secondary Cities**

## Intergenerational bargaining / age-structured political economy

- **[S149] OECD — Employment Outlook 2025: Demographic change, economic growth and intergenerational inequalities**  
  Why it matters: ties ageing directly to intergenerational inequality, showing worsening dependency ratios, slower growth, widening wealth gaps, declining youth homeownership, and a shift in poverty risk from older people toward children.

  Why it matters: shows rising house prices and unequal access to homeownership widening wealth gaps between younger and older generations.

- **[S152] IMF — G20 Background Note on the Implications of Aging and Migration on Growth and Productivity**  
  Why it matters: shows ageing as a growth and public-finance problem that can crowd out more growth-supportive investment unless labour supply and entitlement systems adjust.

- **[S153] OECD — More Effective Social Protection for Stronger Economic Growth (RTM 2024 findings)**  
  Why it matters: provides evidence on how the public already interprets ageing as a policy problem, including concern about ageing, support for labour-supply responses, and weaker enthusiasm for simple pronatalist measures.

- **[S154] OECD — Cities for All Ages**  
  Why it matters: shows age-structured bargaining appearing in urban systems through housing, transport, public space, and age-inclusive local development.

- **[S160] FAA — NextGen Final Report, 2025**

## Anticipation / forecasting / hidden bureaucracy

- **[S175] WHO — Epidemic Intelligence from Open Sources (EIOS) initiative and strategy 2024–2026**  
  Why it matters: shows WHO treating open-source epidemic intelligence as a core part of how public-health intelligence is generated and applied so decision-makers can act earlier.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/initiatives/eios

- **[S176] ECDC — Future challenges for infectious disease prevention and control**  
  Why it matters: shows a formal strategic-foresight process, beginning with horizon scanning, being used to shape public-health preparedness, research, training, and long-term planning.  
  URL: https://www.ecdc.europa.eu/en/publications-data/future-challenges-infectious-disease-prevention-and-control

- **[S177] EMA — Network Data Steering Group workplan 2025–2028**  
  Why it matters: shows medicines regulators explicitly horizon scanning and monitoring research and innovation projects to keep abreast of emerging data tools and regulatory needs.  
  URL: https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/other/network-data-steering-group-workplan-2025-2028_en.pdf

- **[S178] OECD — Strategic intelligence tools for emerging technology governance**  
  Why it matters: treats strategic intelligence as a concrete policy toolkit for anticipatory governance of emerging technologies rather than a speculative luxury.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/strategic-intelligence-tools-for-emerging-technology-governance_02c05775-en.html

- **[S179] OECD — AI in strategic foresight**  
  Why it matters: shows AI already being used by foresight practitioners for signal scanning, data analysis, and scenario development, while also documenting adoption constraints in the public sector.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/ai-in-strategic-foresight_aa573076-en.html

## Thermal geography / coolth / heat resilience

- **[S180] ECMWF — 2025 was the third-warmest year on record, Copernicus data show**  
  Why it matters: shows strong heat stress becoming more widespread across land areas rather than remaining a rare extreme, supporting the claim that thermal stress is becoming a more normal background condition.  
  URL: https://www.ecmwf.int/en/about/media-centre/news/2025/2025-third-warmest-year

- **[S181] WHO / WMO — Report and guidance to protect workers from increasing heat stress**  
  Why it matters: frames heat as a labour-capacity and productivity issue, not just a comfort issue, and gives an institutional basis for heat-safe workability becoming a real bottleneck.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/news/item/22-08-2025-who-wmo-issue-new-report-and-guidance-to-protect-workers-from-increasing-heat-stress

- **[S182] World Bank / GFDRR — Extreme Heat Could Cost Cities in Europe and Central Asia 2.5% of GDP Annually by 2050**  
  Why it matters: shows heat moving into urban macroeconomics, with implications for infrastructure renewal, labour productivity, health systems, and city competitiveness.  
  URL: https://www.worldbank.org/en/news/press-release/2025/06/23/extreme-heat-could-cost-cities-in-europe-and-central-asia-2-5-of-gdp-annually-by-2050

- **[S183] IEA — Data centres and data transmission networks**  
  Why it matters: shows thermal conditions and water stress entering the siting logic of data infrastructure, making coolth relevant to compute geography and not only to human comfort.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/energy-system/buildings/data-centres-and-data-transmission-networks

- **[S184] U.S. Department of Energy — Cooling Water Efficiency Opportunities for Federal Data Centers**  
  Why it matters: shows that many cooling systems are water-intensive and that thermal management choices are materially constrained by water use and cooling efficiency.  
  URL: https://www.energy.gov/cmei/femp/cooling-water-efficiency-opportunities-federal-data-centers

- **[S185] FAO / WMO — Extreme heat risks for agriculture**  
  Why it matters: extends the thermal-constraint thesis into food systems, labour exposure, and crop productivity rather than leaving it as an urban or compute-only issue.  
  URL: https://wmo.int/media/news/fao-and-wmo-report-highlights-extreme-heat-risks-agriculture


## Water entitlements / allocation / basin politics

- **[S186] World Bank Group — Building a Water-Secure Future (FY25–30 Water Strategy)**  
  Why it matters: frames water security as a cross-sector development constraint and states that planetary boundaries for green and blue water have been crossed.  
  URL: https://thedocs.worldbank.org/en/doc/ad77c384b22e0d83d2a8e5e850e063e3-0400022025/related/WBGWaterStrategy.pdf

- **[S187] OECD — Water Resources Allocation**  
  Why it matters: defines allocation as the rule-set that determines who may use water, how, when, and where, making allocation itself a first-class governance variable.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/water-resources-allocation_9789264229631-en.html

- **[S188] UN-Water — Progress on the level of water stress (2024 update)**  
  Why it matters: shows that high water stress signals unsustainable withdrawals and intense competition across sectors, linking scarcity directly to allocation conflict.  
  URL: https://www.unwater.org/sites/default/files/2024-12/SDG6_Indicator_Report_642_Progress-of-water-stress_2024_EN.pdf

- **[S189] UN-Water — Progress on Transboundary Water Cooperation (2024 update)**  
  Why it matters: shows that many shared basins still lack robust operational arrangements, making cross-border allocation and adaptation a live governance bottleneck.  
  URL: https://www.unwater.org/sites/default/files/2024-09/SDG6_Indicator_Report_652_Progress-on-Transboundary-Water-Cooperation_2024_EN.pdf

- **[S190] IEA — Energy and Water**  
  Why it matters: shows that water allocation is now entangled with energy production and that energy systems are themselves necessary for water supply and treatment.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/topics/energy-and-water

- **[S191] IEA — Wired for water: How electrification is transforming desalination**  
  Why it matters: shows rising scarcity pushing desalination and ties water security more tightly to electricity demand, infrastructure, and industrial planning.  
  URL: https://www.iea.org/commentaries/wired-for-water-how-electrification-is-transforming-desalination

- **[S192] World Bank / 2030 WRG — WRG Strategy 2026 to 2030**  
  Why it matters: explicitly treats water allocation as part of the public-side reform package needed to unlock investment and scalable water-security solutions.  
  URL: https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099021826120079617/pdf/P181147-3f874100-002a-4768-bc8a-fffa3f041418.pdf


## Urban ecology / place biology / environmental exposure

- **[S193] WHO — Urban health fact sheet**  
  Why it matters: shows WHO already treating healthy urban planning and the environmental management of vector-borne diseases as part of mainstream urban-health work.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/urban-health

- **[S194] WHO — Urban planning: health risks**  
  Why it matters: states that city design shapes exposure to environmental factors and explicitly links unhealthy urban environments to vector-borne, waterborne, respiratory, and sanitation-related disease risks.

- **[S195] WHO / UN-Habitat — Integrating health in urban and territorial planning: a sourcebook**  
  Why it matters: institutionalises the planning-health interface by giving urban leaders, planners, and health professionals a shared framework for incorporating health into territorial planning.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/9789240003170

- **[S196] WHO — Health in urban planning resource directory**  
  Why it matters: shows WHO maintaining an updated operational library of evidence and tools for integrating health-specific concerns into urban planning practice.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/tools/health-in-urban-planning-resource-directory

- **[S197] WHO — Global framework for the response to malaria in urban areas**  
  Why it matters: explicitly treats malaria and other vector-borne disease response as something city leaders, health programmes, and urban planners must handle together using local data.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/9789240061781

- **[S198] WHO / UN-Habitat — Responding to malaria in urban areas: a new framework from WHO and UN-Habitat**  
  Why it matters: states plainly that malaria prevention in cities should be embedded in urban development plans and that housing, piped water, drainage, and cleaner environments are fundamental disease-control tools.

- **[S199] ECDC — Guidance for assessing and mitigating the risk of locally-acquired Aedes-borne viral diseases in the EU/EEA**  
  Why it matters: shows climate-sensitive vector risk being operationalised geographically through environmental, entomological, and epidemiological conditions, pushing local ecology into preparedness and planning.

- **[S200] European Climate and Health Observatory / Climate-ADAPT — Pollen**  
  Why it matters: shows allergen exposure becoming a climate-and-city-design issue, with urban heat islands and changing climate affecting pollen timing, concentration, and health burdens.  
  URL: https://climate-adapt.eea.europa.eu/en/observatory/topics/health-impacts/pollen

- **[S201] WHO — Protecting Health and Biodiversity through Nature-based Solutions**  
  Why it matters: argues that countries should identify environmental risk factors for disease and assess nature-based solutions as part of health protection, linking biodiversity choices to disease and resilience management.  
  URL: https://cdn.who.int/media/docs/default-source/climate-change/nbs-for-health_policy-brief.pdf

- **[S202] UN-Habitat — Strategic Plan for the period 2026–2029**  
  Why it matters: shows UN-Habitat explicitly advancing urban planning and the mainstreaming of biodiversity and ecosystem services into urban and territorial planning and management.  
  URL: https://unhabitat.org/sites/default/files/2025/03/strategic_plan_for_the_period_2026-2029_corr_rf.pdf

- **[S203] UN-Habitat — Resolution 2/4: Biodiverse and resilient cities**  
  Why it matters: shows biodiversity and ecosystem services being formalised as an urban and territorial planning agenda rather than left as a peripheral environmental add-on.  
  URL: https://unhabitat.org/unhabitat-resolutions

## Retreat / relocation / buyouts / place exit

- **[S204] World Bank — Rethinking Resilience: Adapting to a Changing Climate (2026)**  
  Why it matters: states plainly that in the highest-risk areas, managed retreat may be the least-cost and most humane option, showing retreat entering mainstream resilience strategy.  
  URL: https://ppp.worldbank.org/sites/default/files/2026-01/Rethinking%20Resilience%20Adapting%20to%20a%20Changing%20Climate.pdf

- **[S205] OECD — Economic Surveys: Canada 2025, adaptation chapter**  
  Why it matters: treats managed retreat, buyouts, and setback zones as live adaptation instruments, indicating retreat is moving into ordinary policy toolkits.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/oecd-economic-surveys-canada-2025_28f9e02c-en/full-report/adapting-to-climate-change-challenges_e62681b8.html

- **[S206] HUD — January 2025 disaster-recovery announcement on the Universal Notice**  
  Why it matters: explicitly describes alignment with FEMA requirements for environmental reviews and community-driven relocation, showing softer operational naming in U.S. disaster policy.

- **[S207] HUD — Community Development Block Grant Disaster Recovery Universal Notice (2025)**  
  Why it matters: states that voluntary buyouts focus activity on areas where relocation plans are community driven, making retreat administratively real through voluntary buyout design.

- **[S208] World Bank — Groundswell Part II: Acting on Internal Climate Migration**  
  Why it matters: treats planned relocation as a complex, last-resort, participatory response, highlighting that institutions accept the need while still surrounding it with strong legitimacy conditions.

- **[S209] World Bank — Building the Resilience of Vulnerable Populations and Communities (2025)**  
  Why it matters: says transformative adaptation remains rare and under-supported, and that few communities benefit from systemic interventions such as planned relocation, indicating a gap between recognition and institutionalization.

- **[S210] OECD — Financial Protection Against Catastrophic Risks: design of flood risk insurance programmes (2026)**  
  Why it matters: shows insurance programmes can make controversial measures such as managed retreat easier to implement, tying retreat to adjacent policy systems rather than overt ideological acceptance.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/financial-protection-against-catastrophic-risks_32a3acd7-en/full-report/the-design-of-flood-risk-insurance-programmes_68c355ee.html

- **[S214] European Commission — Public Procurement of Innovation**

- **[S218] PAHO — Strategic Fund**  
  Why it matters: shows pooled procurement being used to improve value for money, strengthen supply management, and improve access to essential medicines and strategic health supplies.

## Public finance / budgeting / debt service / fiscal room

- **[S219] IMF — Fiscal Monitor: Fiscal Policy under Uncertainty (April 2025)**  
  Why it matters: shows the current fiscal backdrop directly: debt projections revised upward, rising uncertainty, higher defence spending, and the need for credible medium-term adjustment and buffers.  
  URL: https://www.imf.org/en/publications/fm/issues/2025/04/23/fiscal-monitor-april-2025

- **[S220] OECD — Quality Budget Institutions: Set clear fiscal objectives (2025)**  
  Why it matters: shows how debt, interest costs, and ageing are pushing governments toward stronger top-down budgeting, fiscal objectives, and expenditure ceilings.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/quality-budget-institutions_8e811202-en/full-report/set-clear-fiscal-objectives_490cf9b0.html

- **[S221] OECD — Sustainability of public finances in OECD countries (2025)**  
  Why it matters: explicitly frames the problem as a “dual pressure challenge” of restoring fiscal sustainability while handling ageing, defence, climate, and future-crisis buffers, and highlights expenditure rigidity as a central obstacle.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/sustainability-of-public-finances-in-oecd-countries_df89a38a-en.html

- **[S222] IMF — Fiscal Monitor: Spending Smarter (October 2025)**  
  Why it matters: shows that, under a constrained spending envelope, composition and reallocation become decisive; the fiscal question shifts from expanding budgets to ranking claims inside them.  
  URL: https://www.imf.org/en/publications/fm/issues/2025/10/07/fiscal-monitor-october-2025

- **[S223] European Commission — Debt Sustainability Monitor 2025**  
  Why it matters: operationalises long-run fiscal sustainability as a measurable adjustment problem and shows ageing-related expenditure as the main driver of high long-term risk in all high-risk EU cases.  
  URL: https://economy-finance.ec.europa.eu/publications/debt-sustainability-monitor-2025_en

- **[S224] World Bank / FCDO — Public finance done differently (2025)**  
  Why it matters: states plainly that global debt is rising while fiscal space is shrinking and public expectations are broadening, which is the political setting in which triage budgeting becomes more likely.  
  URL: https://www.worldbank.org/en/programs/financial-management-umbrella-program/brief/public-finance-done-differently

## Distributed diagnostics / point-of-care / remote monitoring

- **[S225] WHO — In vitro diagnostics**  
  Why it matters: states that diagnosis is a weak link in the care cascade, that point-of-care tests can optimize treatment and avoid referrals, and that essential diagnostics should exist both at community point of care and in laboratories.

- **[S226] WHO — WHO recommends near point-of-care tests, tongue swabs, and sputum pooling for TB diagnosis (9 March 2026)**

- **[S227] WHO — Rapid diagnostic test accessibility considerations for professional use and self-tests (2025)**  
  Why it matters: states that rapid diagnostic tests have become widespread across laboratories, clinics, pharmacies, and homes, highlighting the outward spread of testing beyond traditional clinical settings.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/9789240108349

- **[S228] WHO — Self-testing implementation toolkit for HIV, hepatitis C and syphilis (2025)**  
  Why it matters: says self-testing increases access, decentralizes services, and alleviates testing burden on facilities while still requiring confirmatory and linkage-to-care pathways.

- **[S229] FDA — Digital Diagnostics: Over-the-Counter (OTC) and Point-of-Care (POC)**  
  Why it matters: states that tests traditionally performed in laboratories are increasingly performed outside labs and that the resulting data-capture and reporting problem is now a regulatory priority.

- **[S230] FDA — At-Home OTC COVID-19 Diagnostic Tests**  
  Why it matters: shows a mature operational template for self-testing without prescription or laboratory submission, plus digital self-reporting of results.

- **[S231] FDA — Medical Devices that Incorporate Sensor-based Digital Health Technology**  
  Why it matters: explicitly states that care has moved from the hospital environment to the home environment and treats real-time, out-of-clinic sensing as part of the current medical-device landscape.  
  URL: https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/digital-health-center-excellence/medical-devices-incorporate-sensor-based-digital-health-technology

- **[S232] CMS — Remote Patient Monitoring**  
  Why it matters: shows Medicare broadly covering patient-collected physiologic data from connected devices for chronic and acute conditions, turning remote sensing into reimbursable routine care.  
  URL: https://www.cms.gov/medicare/coverage/telehealth/remote-patient-monitoring

- **[S233] NHS England — Virtual Ward statistics**  
  Why it matters: shows a national health system treating hospital-at-home capacity as a measurable operational layer rather than an experimental fringe service.

- **[S234] NHS England — Urgent community care: clinical decision-making framework**  
  Why it matters: explicitly says acutely unwell patients can often be managed at home when teams have diagnostics, point-of-care testing, remote monitoring, and clear escalation protocols.  
  URL: https://www.england.nhs.uk/long-read/urgent-community-care-clinical-decision-making-framework/

- **[S236] WHO — Sodium reduction (7 February 2025)**

- **[S239] WHO — Guideline on fortification of edible oils and fats with vitamins A and D for public health (24 October 2025)**

- **[S243] CDC — Obesity Strategies: What Can Be Done (2 December 2025)**

## Ageing / accessibility / mainstream design defaults

- **[S249] WHO — Ageing and health (1 October 2025)**  
  Why it matters: gives the core demographic pressure, stating that by 2030 one in six people in the world will be aged 60 or over and that the 60+ population will reach 2.1 billion by 2050.

- **[S250] WHO — WHO's work on the UN Decade of Healthy Ageing (2021–2030)**  
  Why it matters: makes age-friendly environments one of the Decade’s explicit action areas and frames healthy ageing as requiring changes in how institutions think about age and ageing.  
  URL: https://www.who.int/initiatives/decade-of-healthy-ageing

- **[S251] WHO — Creating age-friendly cities and communities**  
  Why it matters: defines age-friendly places in concrete design terms such as accessible and safe transport, barrier-free buildings and houses, seating, and sanitary facilities.

- **[S252] OECD — Demographic shifts highlight the need for age-inclusive cities (14 April 2025)**

- **[S253] OECD — How to ensure better ageing in place? (8 October 2025)**  
  Why it matters: treats accessibility, affordable transport, and community-based supports as practical conditions for independence and ageing in place rather than as optional amenities.  
  URL: https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/the-economic-benefit-of-promoting-healthy-ageing-and-community-care_0f7bc62b-en/full-report/how-to-ensure-better-ageing-in-place_c4a6cb66.html

- **[S254] European Commission — European Accessibility Act (EAA)**  
  Why it matters: establishes common EU accessibility requirements across key mass-market products and services including computers, smartphones, ticketing/check-in machines, transport services, banking, e-books, and e-commerce.  
  URL: https://commission.europa.eu/strategy-and-policy/policies/justice-and-fundamental-rights/disability/european-accessibility-act-eaa_en

- **[S255] European Commission — The EU becomes more accessible for all (31 July 2025)**  
  Why it matters: spells out that the Act reaches concrete design features such as adequate font size and text-to-speech, showing accessibility moving into ordinary product specification.  
  URL: https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/eu-becomes-more-accessible-all

- **[S256] ADA.gov — State and Local Governments: First Steps Toward Complying with the ADA Title II Web and Mobile Application Accessibility Rule (8 January 2025)**  
  Why it matters: shows web and mobile accessibility becoming an implementation and planning obligation for public entities, with compliance timelines in 2026 and 2027.  
  URL: https://www.ada.gov/resources/web-rule-first-steps/

- **[S257] NHS England — Accessible Information Standard (updated 3 March 2026)**  
  Why it matters: requires NHS and publicly funded adult social care services to ensure disabled people and people with impairments or sensory loss can access and understand information and receive communication support.  
  URL: https://www.england.nhs.uk/publication/accessible-information-standard/


## Service automation / assisted access / human fallback

- **[S258] GOV.UK Service Manual — Assisted digital support: an introduction (updated 18 December 2025)**

- **[S259] GOV.UK Service Manual — Designing assisted digital support**

- **[S260] GOV.UK Service Manual — How your assisted digital support will be assessed**  
  Why it matters: shows fallback channels being audited as part of live digital-service assessment, including free support, sustainable support models, capacity, and end-to-end user journeys.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/helping-people-to-use-your-service/how-your-assisted-digital-support-will-be-assessed

- **[S261] NHS England — Accessible Information Standard (updated 3 March 2026)**  
  Why it matters: requires NHS and publicly funded adult social care services to ensure disabled people and people with impairments or sensory loss can access information and receive the communication support needed to use services.  
  URL: https://www.england.nhs.uk/publication/accessible-information-standard/

- **[S262] CFPB — Chatbots in consumer finance (6 June 2023)**

- **[S263] CFPB — Issue spotlight on AI chatbots in banking (6 June 2023)**  
  Why it matters: states directly that deficient chatbots that prevent access to live, human support can lead to law violations, diminished service, and other harms.

- **[S264] CFPB — Twelve years of protecting consumers and honest businesses (20 July 2023)**  
  Why it matters: records supervisory concern about banks using chatbots as a cheaper substitute for human customer service while consumers complain they cannot reach a human for more complicated issues.

- **[S265] ADA.gov — Fact Sheet: New Rule on the Accessibility of Web Content and Mobile Apps Provided by State and Local Governments (8 April 2024)**  
  Why it matters: shows that even when some digital-content exceptions apply, public entities still likely need to provide accessible content to people who need it, preserving alternative-access obligations alongside digitization.

- **[S266] EUR-Lex — Directive (EU) 2019/882 on the accessibility requirements for products and services**  
  Why it matters: explicitly reaches support services such as help desks, call centres, technical support, relay services, and training services, showing that assisted channels themselves are part of the accessibility regime.  
  URL: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/PDF/?uri=CELEX:32019L0882

## Plain language / content design / communication clarity

- **[S267] Digital.gov — Plain Language Guide Series**

- **[S270] CDC — The Clear Communication Index**  
  Why it matters: frames communication clarity as something research-based and assessable through scored criteria rather than as a purely stylistic preference.

- **[S271] GOV.UK Service Standard — Make the service simple to use**

- **[S272] Department for Education — Plain Language standard (2 March 2025)**

- **[S273] FCA — Consumer Duty**  
  Why it matters: establishes higher standards of consumer protection across financial services and makes customer understanding a governed outcome rather than a purely voluntary design choice.

- **[S274] FCA — Consumer understanding: good practice and areas for improvement (13 March 2026)**  
  Why it matters: says firms should treat communication design, testing, monitoring, and governance as a coherent end-to-end process, showing comprehension becoming an auditable management surface.

- **[S275] NHS England — Good communication with patients waiting for care**  
  Why it matters: says language should be clear, accessible, and easy to understand, and that acronyms and technical terms should be avoided or explained in patient communications.

- **[S276] NHS England — Accessible Information Standard**  
  Why it matters: requires NHS and publicly funded adult social care services to ensure people can access and understand information and receive the communication support they need to use services.

- **[S277] SEC — Plain English Disclosure**  
  Why it matters: requires key prospectus sections to be written in plain English and explicitly links clearer drafting to better-informed investors.

## Revocation propagation / authority freshness

- **[S429] IRS — Tax Pro Account**

- **[S430] IRS — Power of attorney and other authorizations**  
  Why it matters: lays out multiple revocation and expiry semantics, including automatic revocation of prior authorizations for the same matters and automatic expiration of oral authority after the conversation ends.  
  URL: https://www.irs.gov/businesses/small-businesses-self-employed/power-of-attorney-and-other-authorizations

- **[S431] SSA — POMS DI 31001.001 Representation of Claimants**  
  Why it matters: explicitly describes representative appointment information propagating from RASR to EDCS/DCPS and the removal of the representative flag when a termination is processed.  
  URL: https://secure.ssa.gov/poms.nsf/lnx/0431001001

- **[S432] GOV.UK — Using the donor’s LPA: access codes and View an LPA**  
  Why it matters: says the online LPA view is more up to date than paper, highlights that attorney status can change, and uses organisation-specific 30-day access codes instead of indefinite paper reliance.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/using-the-donors-lpa-access-codes-and-view-an-lpa

- **[S433] GOV.UK — Manage a lasting power of attorney: changes you need to report**  
  Why it matters: shows that authority state can be cancelled or updated after death or attorney change and that replacement attorneys can begin acting when the previous attorney stops.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/manage-lasting-power-attorney/changes-you-need-to-report

- **[S434] NHS England Digital — National proxy service**  
  Why it matters: creates a national proxy data store with legal basis, expiry and review dates, auditable changes, migration from local systems, and an API for other services to rely on.  
  URL: https://digital.nhs.uk/services/national-proxy-service

- **[S435] CMS — Statement on System Changes to Stop Unauthorized Agent and Broker Marketplace Activity**  
  Why it matters: shows CMS responding to stale or mis-bound agent authority by blocking changes unless the agent is already associated with the consumer’s enrollment.  
  URL: https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/press-releases/cms-statement-system-changes-stop-unauthorized-agent-broker-marketplace-activity

- **[S436] CMS — Marketplace Compliance for Agents and Brokers**  
  Why it matters: adds direct-involvement requirements to change the associated agent, limits status checks to affiliated applications, and sets 365-day authorization renewal at the call centre.  
  URL: https://www.cms.gov/files/document/marketplace-compliance-agents-and-brokers.pdf

- **[S437] ATO Software Developers — Using Digital ID, RAM and machine credentials (M2M)**  
  Why it matters: shows expiry, notification, renewal, and revocation logic for machine credentials, including rerouting notices when the original custodian is no longer authorised.  
  URL: https://softwaredevelopers.ato.gov.au/usingdigitalidramandmachinecredentials

- **[S438] Services Australia — Add or cancel someone to act for you for Centrelink or aged care**  
  Why it matters: shows that nominee cancellation is treated as an ongoing state change with immediate cancellation options and notification to both the principal and the former nominee.  
  URL: https://www.servicesaustralia.gov.au/add-or-cancel-someone-to-act-for-you-for-centrelink-or-aged-care?context=22501

- **[S440] GOV.UK — Making public services work for you with your digital identity**

- **[S442] IRS — Manage access in Business Tax Account**

- **[S443] IRS — Submit power of attorney and tax information authorizations**

- **[S444] Services Australia — Centrelink Organisation Nominee Services**

- **[S445] Services Australia — Add or cancel someone to act for you for Centrelink or aged care**

- **[S447] Companies House — List of Authorised Corporate Service Providers (ACSPs)**

- **[S448] ATO Software Developers — Using Digital ID, RAM and machine credentials (M2M)**

- **[S449] CMS — Marketplace Compliance for Agents and Brokers**  
  Why it matters: shows authority checks becoming part of the transaction path by blocking enrollment changes unless the agent is already associated, otherwise requiring a three-way call or approved consumer pathway.

## Authority freshness guarantees / compliance metrics

- **[S450] IRS — 21.2.1 Systems**  
  Why it matters: makes freshness semantics explicit by removing unapproved authorizations after 120 days and distinguishing pending, approved, processing, completed, expired, and withdrawn states.  
  URL: https://www.irs.gov/irm/part21/irm_21-002-001r

- **[S451] GOV.UK — Making public services work for you with your digital identity**  
  Why it matters: says digital-ID attributes must be alterable, credentials reissued when errors are found, and digital IDs updated or reissued periodically for technology changes or expiry dates.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/government/consultations/making-public-services-work-for-you-with-your-digital-identity/making-public-services-work-for-you-with-your-digital-identity

- **[S452] GOV.UK — UK digital identity and attributes trust framework gamma (0.4)**  
  Why it matters: requires account-change and revocation processes and re-verification of inactive identities after 14 months, turning staleness into a managed trust-framework concern.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/uk-digital-identity-and-attributes-trust-framework-04/uk-digital-identity-and-attributes-trust-framework-gamma-04-pre-release

- **[S453] SSA — Representative Payee Program**  
  Why it matters: shows representative authority being kept current through annual reports plus discretionary review and educational-visit mechanisms rather than left indefinitely untouched.  
  URL: https://www.ssa.gov/payee/

- **[S454] SSA — Annual Report on the Results of Periodic Representative Payee Site Reviews and Other Reviews (FY 2024)**  
  Why it matters: exposes a standing review regime with periodic, targeted, educational, state-onsite, and predictive-model review types, making freshness and oversight operationally measurable.  
  URL: https://www.ssa.gov/legislation/Annual%20Rep%20Payee%20Site%20Review%20Report%20%28FY%2024%29.pdf

- **[S455] GOV.UK — Prove you're an attorney on a lasting power of attorney**  
  Why it matters: uses a 30-day access code to prove current attorney status, showing that even legally powerful delegations increasingly rely on short-lived verification artifacts.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/manage-lasting-power-attorney/proving-your-lasting-power-of-attorney

- **[S456] GOV.UK — Request an activation key**  
  Why it matters: shows that the activation key for adding an LPA to an online account expires after a year and must then be reissued, reinforcing the lifecycle logic around authority artifacts.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/guidance/request-an-activation-key

- **[S457] Relationship Authorisation Manager — Manage authorisations**  
  Why it matters: treats express consent itself as a governed-duration object, now valid for up to 7 years, with history export and dashboard management.  
  URL: https://info.authorisationmanager.gov.au/manage-authorisations

- **[S458] Relationship Authorisation Manager — Manage machine credentials**  
  Why it matters: defines 2-year validity, 60/30/7-day expiry notifications, immediate revocation, 60-day suspended-but-active windows for unassigned credentials, and automatic revocation if they are not claimed in time.  
  URL: https://info.authorisationmanager.gov.au/manage-authorisations/resources-for-business-software-users-and-providers/create-and-manage-machine-credentials/manage-machine-credentials

## Offline-verifiable authority artifacts

- **[S487] GOV.UK — Prove you're an attorney on a lasting power of attorney**  
  Why it matters: shows delegated authority becoming a bounded presentation artifact, with a 30-day access code for each organisation plus paper fallback for the same legal mandate.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/manage-lasting-power-attorney/proving-your-lasting-power-of-attorney

- **[S488] AAMVA — Mobile Driver License (mDL) Frequently Asked Questions for Law Enforcement**  
  Why it matters: says both the credential and reader can operate offline, that the data is stored in a secure container on the phone, and that server retrieval is not the recommended default because of privacy implications.  
  URL: https://www.aamva.org/getmedia/6c37bda5-e43d-493e-8294-bba0d18aa2f9/mDL-FAQ-for-LE-01242024.pdf

- **[S489] Google for Developers — In-person Acceptance of Digital Credentials**  
  Why it matters: shows the verifier side becoming general-purpose and lightweight, with ISO 18013-5 credentials accepted by any device that can implement NFC / QR engagement and BLE transfer.  
  URL: https://developers.google.com/wallet/identity/verify/accepting-ids-from-wallet-offline

- **[S490] European Commission — European Digital Identity**  
  Why it matters: states that the EU Digital Identity Wallet will enable online and offline public and private services, digital document sharing, and organisational representation, with rollout due by the end of 2026.  
  URL: https://commission.europa.eu/topics/digital-economy-and-society/european-digital-identity_en

- **[S491] European Commission — EU Digital Identity Wallet: Identification in proximity scenarios**  
  Why it matters: makes face-to-face wallet presentation a first-class use case for hotels, airports, pharmacies, law enforcement, and self-service environments, turning presentation artifacts into everyday service infrastructure.  
  URL: https://ec.europa.eu/digital-building-blocks/sites/spaces/EUDIGITALIDENTITYWALLET/pages/930451396/Identification%2Bin%2Bproximity%2Bscenarios

- **[S492] SMART Health Cards**  
  Why it matters: packages verified clinical information into a secure QR code that can be kept digitally or printed on paper, showing signed artifacts traversing both smartphone and paper channels.  
  URL: https://smarthealth.cards/

- **[S493] W3C — Verifiable Credentials Data Model v2.0**  
  Why it matters: codifies a standard model for cryptographically secure, privacy-respecting, machine-verifiable credentials across many credential types, not only government ID.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-data-model-2.0/

- **[S494] W3C — The Verifiable Credentials 2.0 family of specifications is now a W3C Recommendation**  
  Why it matters: marks the maturing of the standards layer by moving the VC 2.0 family to Recommendation status in May 2025.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/news/2025/the-verifiable-credentials-2.0-family-of-specifications-is-now-a-w3c-recommendation/

- **[S472] myGov — Contact**  
  Why it matters: publishes hotline access and support hours for the gateway that fronts many linked government services, making outage and recovery help part of routine service design.

- **[S473] Services Australia — Customer service changes**

- **[S474] Services Australia — Contact Child Support**

## Graceful degradation / constitutional design

- **[S476] GOV.UK Service Standard — Operate a reliable service**  
  Why it matters: says service teams must minimise downtime and have a plan to deal with it, framing reliability as a user-rights issue rather than a narrow technical concern.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/service-standard/point-14-operate-a-reliable-service

- **[S477] GOV.UK Service Standard — Provide a joined up experience across all channels**  
  Why it matters: says services should meet user needs across online, phone, paper, and face-to-face channels and should have agreed procedures for resolving when online problems affect offline channels or vice versa.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/service-standard/point-3-join-up-across-channels

- **[S478] GOV.UK Service Manual — What happens at a service assessment**  
  Why it matters: tells teams to show what happens when users cannot provide evidence at the right time and to explain the whole journey including offline channels and staff processes, making degraded paths part of service legitimacy.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/service-assessments/how-service-assessments-work

- **[S479] NCSC — Response plan**  
  Why it matters: says organisations should design services to continue operating in a degraded fashion and should plan graceful degradation, scalable fall-back for essential services, and manual alternatives for critical functions.  
  URL: https://www.ncsc.gov.uk/collection/denial-service-dos-guidance-collection/preparing-denial-service-dos-attacks1/response-plan

- **[S480] NCSC — Secure connectivity principles for Operational Technology**  
  Why it matters: asks whether operations can continue manually under degraded or lost connectivity and whether manual intervention procedures are clear and tested, pushing degraded-mode design into operational policy.  
  URL: https://www.ncsc.gov.uk/files/ncsc-secure-connectivity-for-operational-technology.pdf

- **[S481] GOV.UK — Public Emergency Call Service disruption post-incident review**  
  Why it matters: shows that backup procedures and contingency assumptions can diverge across an end-to-end system, making graceful degradation a cross-institutional coordination problem rather than a single-system backup issue.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/public-emergency-call-service-disruption-sunday-25-june-2023-post-incident-review/public-emergency-call-service-disruption-sunday-25-june-2023-post-incident-review

- **[S482] HealthCare.gov — Apply for Health Insurance**  
  Why it matters: states that Marketplace coverage can be applied for online, by phone, and with local help, showing continuity routes built into a high-stakes service rather than left to improvisation.  
  URL: https://www.healthcare.gov/apply-and-enroll/how-to-apply/

- **[S483] HealthCare.gov — Contact us**  
  Why it matters: maintains a 24/7 call center and local-help pathways for Marketplace users, making assisted continuity part of the official service stack.  
  URL: https://www.healthcare.gov/contact-us/

- **[S484] Services Australia — Self service**  
  Why it matters: says people can manage information online or by phone self-service and points those without internet to service-centre terminals and access points, making multi-channel continuity operationally explicit.  
  URL: https://www.servicesaustralia.gov.au/self-service?context=64107

- **[S485] Services Australia — Customer service changes**  
  Why it matters: treats service changes caused by system maintenance, disasters, and closures as a standing public-information problem and routes people to online or phone self-service during disruption.  
  URL: https://www.servicesaustralia.gov.au/customer-service-changes?context=64107

- **[S486] FCA — Operational resilience**  
  Why it matters: requires important business services, impact tolerances, communications plans, vulnerability testing, and recovery learning, showing degraded-mode governance spreading beyond government portals into regulated service infrastructure.  
  URL: https://www.fca.org.uk/firms/operational-resilience


## Indoor air / breathable buildings / cleaner-air infrastructure

- **[S496] WHO — Strategic approach on air quality, energy access and health**  
  Why it matters: situates the 2025 updated road map and states the broader air-health framing, including the 2040 target and WHO’s continuing clean-air strategy.

- **[S497] U.S. EPA — Indoor Air Quality (IAQ)**

- **[S498] U.S. EPA — Wildfires and Indoor Air Quality in Schools and Commercial Buildings**  
  Why it matters: explicitly frames schools and commercial buildings as sites for smoke protection, cleaner-air spaces, and dedicated smoke-event operating practices.

- **[S499] U.S. EPA — Frequent Questions About Wildland Fire Smoke for Community Leaders**  
  Why it matters: operationalizes cleaner-air and cooling centers with guidance on location choice, MERV 13 or higher filtration, portable air cleaners, reduced outdoor-air intake, and PM2.5 sensing.

- **[S500] U.S. Department of Energy — Renew America’s Schools**  
  Why it matters: ties major school energy investments directly to indoor air quality and healthier learning environments rather than treating IAQ as a side issue.  
  URL: https://www.energy.gov/cmei/scep/renew-americas-schools

- **[S501] U.S. Department of Energy — Efficient and Healthy Schools**  
  Why it matters: treats indoor-air upgrades as part of a broader school-improvement and learning-performance agenda, with explicit attention to disadvantaged communities.  
  URL: https://www.energy.gov/cmei/buildings/efficient-and-healthy-schools

- **[S502] U.S. Department of Energy — Prioritizing Heating, Ventilation, and Air Conditioning**  
  Why it matters: says HVAC is integral to learning and health in schools and links ventilation and filtration to a national clean-air-in-buildings agenda.  
  URL: https://www.energy.gov/cmei/buildings/prioritizing-heating-ventilation-and-air-conditioning

- **[S503] CDC — Viral Respiratory Pathogens Toolkit for Nursing Homes**  
  Why it matters: shows ventilation and indoor-air quality becoming part of recurring respiratory management in high-vulnerability care settings, including possible use of in-room HEPA cleaners.  
  URL: https://www.cdc.gov/long-term-care-facilities/hcp/respiratory-virus-toolkit/index.html

- **[S504] ASHRAE — Standard 241, Control of Infectious Aerosols**  
  Why it matters: establishes minimum requirements to reduce disease transmission risk from infectious aerosols across new and existing buildings and major renovations.

- **[S505] NCES / IES — Nearly One-Third of Public Schools Have One or More Portable Buildings in Use**  
  Why it matters: provides current official school-facility context, including that only 39% of public schools reported having an Indoor Air Quality Coordinator.

- **[S506] OSHA — Indoor Air Quality Overview**  
  Why it matters: shows indoor-air responsibilities spreading through workplace safety expectations even without a single comprehensive federal IAQ standard.

- **[S516] FERC — 2025 Assessment of Demand Response and Advanced Metering**
