  URL: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/governance/policy/concepts/exemption-structure

- **[S1329] Microsoft Learn — Get policy compliance data**  
  Why it matters: says policy exemptions being created, updated, or deleted trigger compliance reevaluation, which is direct evidence that exemption-state changes are already treated as meaningful history-bearing events rather than invisible edits.  
  URL: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/governance/policy/how-to/get-compliance-data

- **[S1330] Microsoft Learn — Azure Monitor activity log**  
  Why it matters: says activity log events are retained for 90 days by default and can be routed elsewhere for longer retention, which shows that reconstructable exception lineage already depends on explicit export and retention choices.  
  URL: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/platform/activity-log

- **[S1331] GitHub Docs — Viewing and updating Dependabot alerts**  
  Why it matters: says dismissal comments are added to the alert timeline and can be used as justification during auditing and reporting, which shows that rationale-bearing alert history is already treated as something other institutions may later inspect.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/manage-security-alerts/manage-dependabot-alerts/viewing-and-updating-dependabot-alerts

- **[S1332] GitHub Docs — Audit log events for your organization**  
  Why it matters: lists created, manually dismissed, dismissal-approved, dismissal-denied, reappeared, and reopened code-scanning alert events together with actor, timestamp, alert-number, and dismissal-approver fields, which is unusually strong evidence that extension-adjacent lifecycle history is already a structured audit surface.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/organizations/keeping-your-organization-secure/managing-security-settings-for-your-organization/audit-log-events-for-your-organization

## Gate-expiry disputes / validity-at-crossing services

- **[S1333] Section508.gov — Integrating Section 508 Data into Asset Management and GRC Tools**  
  Why it matters: says procurement teams should verify conformance status and review test reports before award or renewal, require updated accessibility conformance documentation, and reassess IT every 12 to 36 months or based on risk, which shows proof freshness already mattering at specific procurement gates rather than only in abstract compliance posture.  
  URL: https://www.section508.gov/manage/governance/section-508-into-asset-management-and-grc-tools/

- **[S1334] OGC — Compliance Testing Program**  
  Why it matters: says products are removed from the Compliant Products Record after three years unless renewed and that Compliance Certificates expire three years after issuance unless renewed, which is unusually direct evidence that a still-visible compliance claim can have a hard admissibility window.  
  URL: https://portal.ogc.org/files/?artifact_id=7586

- **[S1335] OpenID Foundation — OpenID Federation 1.0**  
  Why it matters: says a Trust Chain expires at the minimum `exp` in the chain, participants must refresh chains when they expire, and validation failures can happen during topology updates, which makes trust validity at the moment of use a first-class operational problem.  
  URL: https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-1_0.html

- **[S1336] W3C — Verifiable Credentials Data Model v2.0**  
  Why it matters: says verifiable credentials can carry validity periods, verification material, and status information, which shows portable proof objects increasingly carrying the metadata needed to dispute whether they were still live at use time.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-data-model-2.0/

- **[S1337] Microsoft Learn — Details of the policy exemption structure**  
  Why it matters: says exemptions can carry an `expiresOn` value and that the exemption object is preserved for record-keeping after expiry but no longer honored, which cleanly separates historical existence from live admissibility.  
  URL: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/governance/policy/concepts/exemption-structure

- **[S1338] GitHub Docs — Deployments and environments**  
  Why it matters: says deployment protection rules can require conditions to pass before a job referencing an environment can proceed and can include manual approvals, delays, or third-party readiness systems, which shows gate passage already becoming a timed and policy-configured workflow event.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/deployments-and-environments

- **[S1339] AWS CodePipeline — Approve or reject an approval action in CodePipeline**  
  Why it matters: says a pipeline stops at a manual approval point, fails if no response is received within seven days, shows source revisions under review, and captures review comments and approval or rejection state, which is direct evidence that gate events already have timeouts, attached revision context, and narrative evidence.  
  URL: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/codepipeline/latest/userguide/approvals-approve-or-reject.html

- **[S1340] GitHub Docs — Reviewing deployments**  
  Why it matters: says pending deployment-protection rules can be approved, rejected, or bypassed and that bypass requires a description before forcing pending jobs to proceed, which shows expiry-adjacent or readiness-adjacent gate failures already being routed into auditable exception paths.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/deploy/configure-and-manage-deployments/review-deployments

- **[S1341] ServiceNow Docs — Request an extension to policy exception**  
  Why it matters: says requesters can ask for extensions more than once, organizations can configure the number of extensions allowed, each request carries an extension date, reason, and justification statement, and approved extension details appear in the Schedule tab, which shows expiry-boundary repair already becoming a structured workflow rather than an informal note.  
  URL: https://www.servicenow.com/docs/r/governance-risk-compliance/policy-and-compliance-management/request-policy-extension-platform.html

- **[S1342] Tenable Vulnerability Management — About Accept Rules**  
  Why it matters: says accept rules can be set to expire so targeted findings reappear and that accepted findings remain findings rather than being rewritten into permanent non-events, which makes expiry at the end of temporary tolerance an operationally visible boundary.  
  URL: https://docs.tenable.com/vulnerability-management/Content/recast/about-accept-rules.htm

- **[S1343] Google Cloud Deploy — Run analysis jobs in Cloud Deploy**  
  Why it matters: says analysis jobs evaluate logs or metrics after deployment and verification and can fail a rollout on bad signals, which shows that a gate crossing can remain contingent on timed post-crossing validation rather than ending with the first proceed action.  
  URL: https://docs.cloud.google.com/deploy/docs/analysis


## Legacy-status transfer attestations / closing artifacts

- **[S1344] eCFR — 40 CFR § 122.61, Transfer of permits**  
  Why it matters: says an NPDES permit may be transferred to a new owner or operator only through modification/reissuance or a qualifying automatic transfer, and that automatic transfer requires advance notice plus a written agreement naming the date for transfer of permit responsibility, coverage, and liability, which makes transfer survival a formal handoff object rather than an assumption.  
  URL: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-40/chapter-I/subchapter-D/part-122/subpart-D/section-122.61

- **[S1345] eCFR — 40 CFR Part 122, Subpart C, Permit Conditions / Transfers**  
  Why it matters: says a permit is not transferable except after notice to the Director and that the Director may require modification or revocation and reissuance to change the permittee, which shows that owner/operator change can break simple continuity unless the transfer path is itself documented.  
  URL: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-40/chapter-I/subchapter-D/part-122/subpart-C

- **[S1346] Connecticut DEEP — Permit Transfer Fact Sheet**  
  Why it matters: says no person may act under a DEEP license issued to someone else unless the license has been transferred, the transfer form is for ownership or operator changes, and a transferred permit only authorizes activities covered by the pre-existing permit, which cleanly separates status handoff from new operational scope.  
  URL: https://portal.ct.gov/DEEP/Permits-and-Licenses/Permitting-Factsheets/Permit-Transfer-Fact-Sheet

- **[S1347] Fannie Mae Multifamily Guide — Zoning and Legally Non-Conforming Status**  
  Why it matters: requires lenders to identify current zoning or land-use designation, determine whether the existing use is expressly permitted, confirm whether property characteristics conform or are legally non-conforming, and deliver structured zoning-report data when a zoning consultant is used, which shows legacy status already becoming loan-file data rather than background narrative.  
  URL: https://mfguide.fanniemae.com/node/18896

- **[S1348] Fannie Mae Multifamily Guide — Legal Non-Conforming Use**  
  Why it matters: requires a legal non-conforming-status loan modification, confirmation of the casualty destruction threshold at which rebuilding to pre-casualty use and condition would be prohibited, and non-delivery of the mortgage loan if the threshold is less than 50%, which makes nonconforming-status survival directly financeable or disqualifying.  
  URL: https://mfguide.fanniemae.com/node/19611

- **[S1349] City of New Haven — Zoning Compliance Letters / Rebuild-Reconstruction Letters**  
  Why it matters: says developers, lawyers, realtors, lenders, potential purchasers, and others often require zoning classification for due diligence, while the standard letter does not verify building or use status, certify violations, or authorize expansion of a nonconforming use or structure, which exposes the gap between advisory classification and operative transfer assurance.  
  URL: https://www.newhavenct.gov/government/departments-divisions/city-plan/zoning-compliance-rebuild-reconstruction-letters

- **[S1350] City of Seven Hills, Ohio — Determination of Legal Nonconforming Use Status**  
  Why it matters: requires evidence that the lot, building, structure, or use was lawfully created, requires the resulting permit to specify the reason and extent of nonconformance, and says sale or transfer does not affect abandonment or discontinuance status, which makes transfer survival tied to documented scope.  
  URL: https://codelibrary.amlegal.com/codes/sevenhills/latest/sevenhills_oh/0-0-0-46812

- **[S1351] City of Scott City, Kansas — Certificate of Nonconformity**  
  Why it matters: says a previously legal conforming use is a vested property right that runs with the land and cannot be lost from sale or transfer, which is unusually direct evidence that legacy status can become a transfer-relevant property attribute.  
  URL: https://codelibrary.amlegal.com/codes/scottcityks/latest/scottcity_ks/0-0-0-3571

- **[S1352] City of Reading, Pennsylvania — Certificate of Nonconforming Use or Structure**  
  Why it matters: lets an owner secure a certificate after providing evidence that the nonconformity is lawful, certifies the right to continue within applicable limits, and keeps copies with the zoning administrator, which shows status proof becoming an official file object.  
  URL: https://codelibrary.amlegal.com/codes/readingpa/latest/reading_pa/0-0-0-24037

- **[S1353] City of Quincy, Illinois — Registration of Nonconforming Use, Buildings and Structures**  
  Why it matters: says a certificate of nonconformance is issued to the property, a future certificate is not required even if ownership changes, and certified copies suitable for recording can be provided, which looks very close to a portable closing artifact.  
  URL: https://codelibrary.amlegal.com/codes/quincy/latest/quincy_il/0-0-0-12554

- **[S1354] City of Horseshoe Bend, Idaho — Nonconforming Uses**  
  Why it matters: says the right to maintain a nonconforming use runs with the land and is not terminated by ownership change, provided the use is not enlarged, expanded, or altered, which makes transfer survival conditional on post-transfer conduct.  
  URL: https://codelibrary.amlegal.com/codes/horseshoebendid/latest/horseshoebend_id/0-0-0-2276

## Renewal-history normalization / lineage brokers

- **[S1355] NIST OSCAL — Plan of Action and Milestones Model**  
  Why it matters: says the OSCAL POA&M model provides structured, machine-readable XML, JSON, and YAML representations of POA&M information, including individual items with risk information, remediation planning, status, and deviation information such as false positives and risk acceptance, which makes remediation history explicitly portable enough to need crosswalks.  
  URL: https://pages.nist.gov/OSCAL/learn/concepts/layer/assessment/poam/

- **[S1356] Open Cybersecurity Schema Framework — Welcome to OCSF**  
  Why it matters: says OCSF provides an extensible framework with a vendor-agnostic core security schema and that data engineers can map differing schemas to simplify ingestion and normalization, which directly supports the thesis that cross-tool semantic mapping becomes its own service layer.  
  URL: https://ocsf.io/

- **[S1357] AWS Documentation — What is Amazon Security Lake?**  
  Why it matters: says Security Lake centralizes security data from AWS, SaaS, on-premises, cloud, and third-party sources, converts it to Parquet, and normalizes it to OCSF so multiple security solutions can consume the same data in parallel, which is a concrete example of normalization becoming managed infrastructure.  
  URL: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/security-lake/latest/userguide/what-is-security-lake.html

- **[S1358] ServiceNow Docs — Table API**  
  Why it matters: says the Table API provides endpoints for create, read, update, and delete operations on existing ServiceNow tables when callers have the required roles, which shows that native records can become programmatically extractable inputs to a renewal-history broker rather than only UI artifacts.  
  URL: https://www.servicenow.com/docs/r/washingtondc/api-reference/rest-apis/c_TableAPI.html

- **[S1359] GitHub Docs — Exporting audit log activity for your enterprise**  
  Why it matters: says enterprise owners can export audit and Git events, audit-log exports can be JSON or CSV, audit events are retained for 180 days, Git events for seven days, and exports include fields such as action, actor, organization, repository, timestamp, and event data, which makes retention windows and event schemas part of the normalization problem.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/admin/monitoring-activity-in-your-enterprise/reviewing-audit-logs-for-your-enterprise/exporting-audit-log-activity-for-your-enterprise

- **[S1360] GitHub Docs — Streaming the audit log for your enterprise**  
  Why it matters: says enterprise audit-log streams export audit and Git event data as compressed JSON files, can be paused or deleted, include seven-day buffer semantics, use at-least-once delivery, and may duplicate events, which shows why normalized history packets need replay and deduplication rules.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/admin/monitoring-activity-in-your-enterprise/reviewing-audit-logs-for-your-enterprise/streaming-the-audit-log-for-your-enterprise

- **[S1361] Atlassian Developer — Jira Cloud REST API, Get changelogs**  
  Why it matters: says Jira returns a paginated list of all changelogs for an issue sorted by date from oldest forward, which makes issue history extractable while still leaving cross-issue and cross-tool normalization to another layer.  
  URL: https://developer.atlassian.com/cloud/jira/platform/rest/v3/api-group-issues/

- **[S1362] Microsoft Learn — Use the Microsoft Graph security API**  
  Why it matters: says Microsoft Graph security APIs aggregate alert and incident data from Microsoft and integrated security providers and can sync the status of specific alerts across integrated security products, which shows normalization and status synchronization already being framed as a platform capability.  
  URL: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/security-api-overview?view=graph-rest-1.0

- **[S1363] Google Cloud Documentation — Stream findings to BigQuery for analysis**  
  Why it matters: says Security Command Center can stream new and updated findings to BigQuery, and that updates create multiple records with the same source and finding identifiers but different event times so analysts can view how finding state changes over time, which is strong evidence for event-time lineage and duplicate-handling requirements.  
  URL: https://docs.cloud.google.com/security-command-center/docs/how-to-analyze-findings-in-big-query

- **[S1364] Google Cloud Documentation — Export Security Command Center data**  
  Why it matters: says findings can be exported as JSON, JSONL, or CSV files and filtered before export, which shows that one-time native exports are available but still need transformation into comparable renewal-history packets.  
  URL: https://docs.cloud.google.com/security-command-center/docs/how-to-export-data

- **[S1365] OASIS Open — Common Security Advisory Framework Version 2.0**  
  Why it matters: defines CSAF as a JSON language for creating, updating, and interoperably exchanging structured security advisories about products, vulnerabilities, impact status, and remediation, which shows adjacent product-status and remediation-status evidence already moving into interoperable formats.  
  URL: https://www.oasis-open.org/standard/common-security-advisory-framework-version-2-0/

- **[S1366] CycloneDX — Security Use Case: Vulnerability Exploitability**  
  Why it matters: says CycloneDX vulnerability-exploitability data captures risk ratings, reproducibility, affected-component information, and exploitability context, which shows why normalized remediation and exception histories increasingly need context fields rather than just binary affected/not-affected labels.  
  URL: https://cyclonedx.org/use-cases/vulnerability-exploitability/

## Normalization-loss warranties / loss-bearing crosswalks

- **[S1367] HL7 FHIR R5 — ConceptMap Resource**  
  Why it matters: describes mappings as one-way, context-dependent relationships between source and target concepts, warns that reverse mappings cannot be assumed, supports multiple possible mappings and no-map cases, and distinguishes ConceptMap relationship assertions from executable StructureMap transforms, which makes semantic loss and reliance purpose explicit enough to become warrantable.  
  URL: https://hl7.org/fhir/R5/conceptmap.html

- **[S1368] W3C — SKOS Simple Knowledge Organization System Primer**  
  Why it matters: distinguishes exact equivalence, inexact equivalence, partial equivalence, broader/narrower relationships, and single-to-multiple mapping cases, while separating `skos:exactMatch` from `skos:closeMatch`, which gives normalization-loss schedules a ready-made language for non-identical semantic correspondence.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/skos-primer/

- **[S1369] W3C — PROV Overview**  
  Why it matters: frames provenance as information about entities, activities, and people involved in producing data that can support assessments of quality, reliability, or trustworthiness, and emphasizes derivation, versioning, reproducibility, and procedures, which is the evidence substrate for warranting a normalized packet.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-overview/

- **[S1370] NIST OSCAL — Profile Resolution**  
  Why it matters: defines import, merge, and modify processing for OSCAL profiles, including setting parameters and altering controls, and requires continued processing with warnings in some missing-target cases, which shows control-baseline transformation already producing explicit transformation effects and warnings.  
  URL: https://pages.nist.gov/OSCAL/learn/concepts/processing/profile-resolution/

- **[S1371] AWS Documentation — Collecting data from custom sources in Security Lake**  
  Why it matters: requires custom sources to convert logs and events to OCSF and meet requirements for source structure, Parquet format, event-class consistency, partitioning, UTC conversion, sorting, and OCSF validation, which turns normalization into a documented conformance obligation rather than a casual export step.  
  URL: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/security-lake/latest/userguide/custom-sources.html

- **[S1372] AWS Security Blog — Transform security logs into OCSF format using a configuration-driven ETL solution**  
  Why it matters: lays out mapping steps for custom logs into OCSF, including field mapping, use of unmapped objects, enrichment, testing, validation, correction of schema misalignments, and rerunning transformations, which is direct evidence that loss and correction points appear inside the mapping workflow.  
  URL: https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/security/transform-security-logs-into-ocsf-format-using-a-configuration-driven-etl-solution/

- **[S1373] OpenLineage — Facets & Extensibility**  
  Why it matters: says facets attach contextual metadata to runs, jobs, and input/output datasets, and that custom facets need distinct prefixes and immutable versioned schema URLs, which shows transformation metadata becoming a governed, versioned attachment rather than an informal note.  
  URL: https://openlineage.io/docs/spec/facets/

- **[S1374] OpenLineage — Object Model**  
  Why it matters: says OpenLineage observes datasets as they are created and transformed, including runtime job-run state and design-time job metadata, which supports treating transformation histories and recipes as evidence that can travel with normalized packets.  
  URL: https://openlineage.io/docs/spec/object-model/

- **[S1375] JSON Schema — Validation: A Vocabulary for Structural Validation of JSON**  
  Why it matters: specifies vocabulary for describing JSON document meaning and making assertions about what a valid document must look like, which supports validation packets attached to normalized diligence artifacts.  
  URL: https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/json-schema-validation

- **[S1376] W3C — Shapes Constraint Language (SHACL)**  
  Why it matters: defines a language for validating RDF graphs against shapes, including validation reports, conformance results, result paths, source shapes, constraint components, messages, and severity, which shows how normalized semantic objects can carry machine-readable validation evidence and failure details.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/shacl/

- **[S1377] dbt Developer Hub — Add data tests to your DAG**  
  Why it matters: frames data tests as assertions about models, sources, seeds, and snapshots, with failing records disproving assertions and custom tests capturing organization-specific business logic, which points to diligence packets carrying test results rather than only transformed data.  
  URL: https://docs.getdbt.com/docs/build/data-tests


## Source-object identity warranties / broker join liability

- **[S1378] W3C — PROV-DM: The PROV Data Model**  
  Why it matters: defines provenance relations such as SpecializationOf and AlternateOf between entities and notes that two provenance descriptions about the same thing may emphasize different aspects, which gives cross-system same-subject assertions a formal conceptual substrate rather than treating them as informal joins.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-dm/

- **[S1379] NIST OSCAL — Identifier Use and UUIDs**  
  Why it matters: says identifiers play a critical role in referencing and linking data consistently and reliably across OSCAL models, which supports treating source-object identity as a first-class evidence problem in control, assessment, and POA&M packets.  
  URL: https://pages.nist.gov/OSCAL/learn/concepts/identifier-use/

- **[S1380] NIST OSCAL Reference — Plan of Action and Milestones Model v1.1.2 JSON Format Reference**  
  Why it matters: describes UUIDs for POA&M instances, observations, findings, assets, risks, and POA&M items, including per-subject consistency across revisions in many definitions, which shows that remediation evidence depends on stable object handles before it can be normalized or warranted.  
  URL: https://pages.nist.gov/OSCAL-Reference/models/v1.1.2/plan-of-action-and-milestones/json-reference/

- **[S1381] ServiceNow Documentation — Unique record identifier (sys_id)**  
  Why it matters: says a new record is assigned a `sys_id` once inserted and that the `sys_id` can be accessed through a record URL or script, which makes native ServiceNow object identity an exportable input to cross-tool identity warranties.  
  URL: https://www.servicenow.com/docs/r/xanadu/platform-administration/c_UniqueRecordIdentifier.html

- **[S1382] Atlassian Developer — Jira Cloud REST API, issue and changelog endpoints**  
  Why it matters: says Jira issues are identified by ID or key, returns changelogs for an issue, and supports bulk changelog fetching for multiple issues sorted by changelog date and issue IDs, which shows that ticket identity and history identity are available but still need careful cross-tool joining.  
  URL: https://developer.atlassian.com/cloud/jira/platform/rest/v2/api-group-issues/

- **[S1383] GitHub Docs — REST API endpoints for Dependabot alerts**  
  Why it matters: says `alert_number` identifies a Dependabot alert within its repository and that the alert state and dismissal metadata travel through the API, which makes repository-local alert identity a concrete but scoped object handle for normalized remediation packets.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependabot/alerts

- **[S1384] Google Cloud Documentation — Managing findings using the Security Command Center API**  
  Why it matters: shows findings being created under a source with fields such as finding name, source ID, state, event time, and resource name, which makes source/finding/resource identity a necessary spine for relating cloud findings to tickets, exceptions, and remediation evidence.  
  URL: https://docs.cloud.google.com/security-command-center/docs/how-to-api-create-manage-findings

- **[S1385] Microsoft Learn — alert resource type, Microsoft Graph v1.0**  
  Why it matters: describes Microsoft Graph security alerts with alert IDs, incident IDs, evidence collections, classifications, determinations, detector IDs, and incident correlation, which shows that security events already carry multiple related identity surfaces that brokers must not collapse casually.  
  URL: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/security-alert?view=graph-rest-1.0

- **[S1386] AWS Documentation — Open Cybersecurity Schema Framework (OCSF) in Security Lake**  
  Why it matters: says OCSF provides a standard schema, versioning criteria, and source-identification fields such as product name, vendor, feature, class name, and schema version, which helps normalize event origin while leaving same-object assertions across systems as a separate broker responsibility.  
  URL: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/security-lake/latest/userguide/open-cybersecurity-schema-framework.html

- **[S1387] OpenLineage — About OpenLineage**  
  Why it matters: says OpenLineage defines dataset, job, and run entities uniquely identified using consistent naming strategies, with an extensible facet model for enrichment, which shows identity discipline becoming part of lineage interoperability.  
  URL: https://openlineage.io/docs/

- **[S1388] SLSA — Provenance v1.0**  
  Why it matters: says provenance is verifiable information about where, when, and how software artifacts were produced, identifies output artifacts through `subject`, and uses resource descriptors with URI, digest, name, and builder identity, which demonstrates artifact-identity evidence that can travel into diligence packets.  
  URL: https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.0/provenance

- **[S1389] CycloneDX — v1.7 XML Reference**  
  Why it matters: defines `bom-ref` as an identifier used to reference an object elsewhere in the BOM and requires every `bom-ref` to be unique within the BOM, which shows local packet identity becoming a prerequisite for dependency graphs, vulnerability claims, and cross-BOM references.  
  URL: https://cyclonedx.org/docs/latest/xml/

## Transformation-code escrow / packet custody

- **[S1390] dbt Developer Hub — About dbt artifacts**  
  Why it matters: says dbt generates JSON artifacts such as `semantic_manifest.json`, `manifest.json`, `catalog.json`, `run_results.json`, and `sources.json` with invocations, and that they support documentation, state, source freshness, test coverage, longitudinal timing analysis, and table-structure history, which makes transform byproducts plausible custody objects.  
  URL: https://docs.getdbt.com/reference/artifacts/dbt-artifacts

- **[S1391] dbt Developer Hub — Manifest JSON file**  
  Why it matters: says `manifest.json` contains a full representation of a dbt project’s resources, including configurations, resource properties, nodes, sources, macros, parent maps, child maps, and compiled SQL for executed nodes, which shows why transform-code escrow may need project manifests rather than only output packets.  
  URL: https://docs.getdbt.com/reference/artifacts/manifest-json

- **[S1392] Great Expectations — Checkpoint**  
  Why it matters: says a checkpoint uses configuration to determine what data to validate, which expectation suites to run, which actions to perform on validation results, and which runtime parameters are supplied, which supports treating validation configuration as part of a replayable packet-custody bundle.  
  URL: https://docs.greatexpectations.io/docs/0.18/reference/learn/terms/checkpoint/

- **[S1393] OpenLineage — Facets & Extensibility**  
  Why it matters: says facets describe what activity ran, how it ran, and what inputs were used, and that custom facets carry schema URLs that should point to immutable versioned schema pointers, which makes lineage metadata and facet-schema pinning part of transform custody.  
  URL: https://openlineage.io/docs/spec/facets/

- **[S1394] OpenLineage — Working with Schemas**  
  Why it matters: says the OpenLineage spec is based on JSON Schema, that schema changes require version bumps, and that generated client code depends on those versions, which shows why replayable packet production needs schema-version retention rather than only current schemas.  
  URL: https://openlineage.io/docs/spec/schemas/

- **[S1395] in-toto Attestation — Link Predicate**  
  Why it matters: describes a link attestation for a supply-chain step with subject products, input materials, step name, command, environment, and byproducts, which provides a ready analogy for signed transform-run attestations around diligence-packet production.  
  URL: https://github.com/in-toto/attestation/blob/main/spec/predicates/link.md

- **[S1396] SLSA — Build: Requirements for producing artifacts**  
  Why it matters: distinguishes provenance completeness, authenticity, accuracy, isolation strength, trusted control-plane generation, external parameters, resolved dependencies, and tamper resistance, which shows how artifact consumers can care about the production method, not only the artifact.  
  URL: https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.2/build-requirements

- **[S1397] Reproducible Builds — Definitions**  
  Why it matters: defines reproducibility as the ability for another party to recreate bit-for-bit identical artifacts from the same source code, build environment, and build instructions, and names source revisions, dependencies, flags, environment variables, and hashes as relevant evidence.  
  URL: https://reproducible-builds.org/docs/definition/

- **[S1398] Reproducible Builds — Recording the build environment**  
  Why it matters: says relevant build-environment information should be defined by the development process or recorded during the build, often as a separate build product such as `buildinfo`, which supports preserving transform-run metadata as a separate custody artifact.  
  URL: https://reproducible-builds.org/docs/recording/

- **[S1399] GitHub Docs — Artifact attestations**  
  Why it matters: says artifact attestations create provenance and integrity claims for software builds, include workflow, repository, environment, commit, and triggering-event information, and require consumers to verify and apply their own policy, which suggests a verification pattern for normalized packet production.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/artifact-attestations

- **[S1400] Sigstore — Bundle Format**  
  Why it matters: defines a Sigstore bundle as the material required to verify a signature on an artifact, including verification material, signature content, transparency-log entries, and timestamps, which points to how transform-run attestations can remain verifiable after the fact.  
  URL: https://docs.sigstore.dev/about/bundle/

- **[S1401] Apache Airflow Documentation — DAG Serialization**  
  Why it matters: says DAG serialization establishes a versioned contract between Task SDKs and Airflow server components and separates orchestration from user-code environments, which shows why pipeline definitions and default-resolution behavior can become replay-relevant custody material.  
  URL: https://airflow.apache.org/docs/apache-airflow/stable/administration-and-deployment/dag-serialization.html

- **[S1402] NIST OSCAL — Assessment Results Model**  
  Why it matters: defines structured, machine-readable assessment results that identify what was assessed, how, by whom, with what findings, observations, risks, evidence, expiration, context, and back matter, which provides a governance pattern for assessing the transform run behind a normalized packet.  
  URL: https://pages.nist.gov/OSCAL/learn/concepts/layer/assessment/assessment-results/

## Non-comparable-state carve-outs / diligence burden allocation

- **[S1403] HL7 FHIR R5 — ConceptMap Resource**  
  Why it matters: says ConceptMap mappings are one-way, context-specific to a business usage, not automatically reversible, and may require different mappings for different use cases such as billing versus analysis, which supports treating comparability as reliance-purpose-specific rather than universal.  
  URL: https://hl7.org/fhir/R5/conceptmap.html

- **[S1404] HL7 FHIR R5 — ConceptMap `noMap`, relationship, and unmatched semantics**  
  Why it matters: defines relationship values such as `equivalent`, `source-is-narrower-than-target`, `source-is-broader-than-target`, and `not-related-to`; gives `noMap` for cases with no target concept; and treats unmatched concepts as unmatched in the target value set, which makes explicit non-comparability a first-class mapping outcome.  
  URL: https://hl7.org/fhir/R5/conceptmap.html

- **[S1405] W3C — SKOS Simple Knowledge Organization System Reference**  
  Why it matters: distinguishes `exactMatch`, `closeMatch`, `broadMatch`, `narrowMatch`, and `relatedMatch`, and says exact matches are high-confidence interchangeable while close matches are only sometimes interchangeable, which supports exposing equivalence strength instead of collapsing all mappings into one comparable state.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/skos-reference/

- **[S1406] NIST NCCoE — Cybersecurity Capability Maturity Model to NIST Cybersecurity Framework Mapping / OLIR relationship model**  
  Why it matters: describes relationship mappings using subset, intersects, equal, superset, and not-related cases, and ties fulfillment treatment to those relationships, which models how partial overlap can become a burden-allocation question rather than a simple pass/fail mapping.  
  URL: https://www.nccoe.nist.gov/news-insights/cybersecurity-capability-maturity-model-nist-cybersecurity-framework-mapping

- **[S1407] AWS Security Hub Documentation — AWS extension for OCSF**  
  Why it matters: says OCSF schemas can be extended with additional attributes, objects, categories, profiles, and event classes to add domain-specific context and improve interoperability, which shows how native distinctions may be preserved outside a common core schema rather than forced into a lossy target field.  
  URL: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/securityhub/latest/userguide/ocsf-aws-extension.html

- **[S1408] Microsoft Learn — Microsoft Graph Security enum values**  
  Why it matters: includes security enum members such as `unknown` and `unknownFutureValue`, which demonstrates an API design pattern for preserving uncertainty and future values instead of forcing every state into a known current vocabulary.  
  URL: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/enums-security?view=graph-rest-1.0

- **[S1409] OASIS — Common Security Advisory Framework Version 2.0**  
  Why it matters: defines CSAF/VEX product-status fields such as `fixed`, `known_affected`, `known_not_affected`, and `under_investigation`, and requires impact statements for known-not-affected products, which shows status assertions becoming contextual and evidence-bearing rather than simple closed/open labels.  
  URL: https://docs.oasis-open.org/csaf/csaf/v2.0/os/csaf-v2.0-os.html

- **[S1410] CycloneDX — Security Use Case: Vulnerability Exploitability**  
  Why it matters: separates vulnerability `state`, `justification`, and `response`, and gives `not_affected` and mitigating-control justifications as contextual claims, which supports treating source status as purpose-bound evidence rather than automatically comparable closure.  
  URL: https://cyclonedx.org/use-cases/vulnerability-exploitability/

- **[S1411] GitHub Docs — REST API endpoints for Dependabot alerts**  
  Why it matters: exposes Dependabot alert states such as `auto_dismissed`, `dismissed`, `fixed`, and `open`, plus severity, scope, manifest, EPSS, and runtime-risk filters, which shows that source-native security states carry platform-specific meanings that may not map cleanly into a buyer’s target categories.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependabot/alerts

- **[S1412] Tenable Vulnerability Management — About Accept Rules**  
  Why it matters: says accept rules hide findings from active remediation queues while retaining records for audit and compliance, can expire and cause findings to reappear, and do not alter vulnerability scoring, which makes accepted-risk states dangerous to flatten into fixed, closed, or absent findings.  
  URL: https://docs.tenable.com/vulnerability-management/Content/recast/about-accept-rules.htm

- **[S1413] NIST OSCAL — Profile Resolution**  
  Why it matters: defines profile resolution as applying a profile to a catalog to produce a tailored catalog and emphasizes deterministic, repeatable outputs across tools, which provides a governance analogy for derivative comparison states that are neither raw source nor arbitrary target labels.  
  URL: https://pages.nist.gov/OSCAL/learn/concepts/processing/profile-resolution/

## Split/merge correction notices / packet amendment events

- **[S1414] HL7 FHIR — Patient resource duplicate linking and link types**  
  Why it matters: describes `replaced-by`, `replaces`, `refer`, and `seealso` links for duplicate, successor, main-source, and overlapping patient records, showing that mature identity systems preserve relation type rather than simply overwriting an old subject.  
  URL: https://build.fhir.org/patient.html

- **[S1415] HL7 FHIR Administrative Incubator — Patient `$merge` OperationDefinition**  
  Why it matters: defines a source-to-target merge, source inactivation, source/target links, asynchronous processing, downstream reference updates, possible de-duplication, provenance creation, missed-history hazards, and downstream notification options, making explicit why subject merges can require notice outside ordinary polling.  
  URL: https://build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/admin-incubator/en/OperationDefinition-Patient-merge.html

- **[S1416] HL7 FHIR — Provenance Resource**  
  Why it matters: says provenance tracks activities that created, revised, deleted, or signed resource versions, can point to past versions, and requires unambiguous/versioned references when versions matter, which supports treating packet corrections as provenance-bearing amendments.  
  URL: https://build.fhir.org/provenance.html

- **[S1417] GitHub Docs — Issue event types**  
  Why it matters: lists issue and pull-request timeline event types such as `marked_as_duplicate`, `unmarked_as_duplicate`, `renamed`, `transferred`, `merged`, `closed`, and `reopened`, demonstrating that object-relationship corrections already travel as named events rather than only as final state.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/issue-event-types

- **[S1418] GitHub Docs — REST API endpoints for timeline events**  
  Why it matters: says timeline events expose issue and pull-request activity and can be used to display activity or determine who should be notified, which models correction history as an event stream for downstream consumers.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/timeline

- **[S1419] Atlassian Developer — Jira Cloud issue link types**  
  Why it matters: shows issue-link types with inward and outward descriptions such as `Duplicated by` and `Duplicates` and says link types define and report relationships between issues, which supports directional relation labels inside correction notices.  
  URL: https://developer.atlassian.com/cloud/jira/platform/rest/v3/api-group-issue-link-types/

- **[S1420] Atlassian Developer — Jira Cloud webhooks**  
  Why it matters: describes Jira webhooks for issue updates and states that the changelog is an array of changed items supplied for `jira:issue_updated`, which points to a common pattern for sending field-level correction payloads to subscribers.  
  URL: https://developer.atlassian.com/cloud/jira/platform/webhooks/

- **[S1421] NIST NVD — Vulnerability APIs / CVE Change History API**  
  Why it matters: says the CVE Change History API provides transparency and lets users monitor when and why CVEs change, including events such as CVE modification, reanalysis, CPE deprecation remaps, and CWE remaps, which is a direct model for correction-history APIs.  
  URL: https://nvd.nist.gov/developers/vulnerabilities

- **[S1422] NIST NVD — Vulnerability Status**  
  Why it matters: explains that REJECTED CVE records may be duplicates, withdrawn records, incorrect assignments, or administrative errors and should no longer be used while remaining visible so users know the identifier is invalid, which shows why identity corrections need persistent invalidation notices.  
  URL: https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/vulnerability-status

- **[S1423] CSAF Author Guide — Tracking**  
  Why it matters: says CSAF tracking contains lifecycle metadata including change history and current state, while the tracking ID remains constant when a new version is released, which models stable subject identity plus amended versions.  
  URL: https://secvisogram.github.io/secvisogram-documentation/

- **[S1424] CycloneDX v1.7 XML Reference — BOM `version`, `serialNumber`, and `bom-ref`**  
  Why it matters: says a modified BOM should increment its version, systems should use the most recent version when serial numbers match, and BOMs should have unique serial numbers, which supports packet versioning and supersession after correction.  
  URL: https://cyclonedx.org/docs/1.7/xml/

- **[S1425] SPDX Specification 3.0.1 — RelationshipType**  
  Why it matters: defines relationship types between elements and stresses that relationship names should make direction clear, which supports explicit split, merge, duplicate, supersedes, replaces, and related-only labels in packet amendments.  
  URL: https://spdx.github.io/spdx-spec/v3.0.1/model/Core/Vocabularies/RelationshipType/

- **[S1426] IETF RFC 6902 — JSON Patch**  
  Why it matters: defines machine-readable patch operations such as add, remove, replace, move, copy, and test with target paths, offering a compact delta format for expressing exact packet amendments rather than reissuing opaque whole packets.  
  URL: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6902

## Amendment-recipient registries / reliance-graph infrastructure

- **[S1427] HL7 FHIR — Subscription Resource**  
  Why it matters: defines a Subscription as a proactive event-notification request from one system to another, tied to a supported SubscriptionTopic and refined through filters, which models amendment rights as registered topic-and-filter relationships rather than simple contact lists.  
  URL: https://fhir.hl7.org/fhir/subscription.html

- **[S1428] HL7 FHIR — Subscriptions Framework**  
  Why it matters: separates SubscriptionTopic, Subscription, channel, endpoint, and payload details, giving a standard pattern for event topics, delivery channels, and recipient endpoints that packet-amendment infrastructure can imitate.  
  URL: https://fhir.hl7.org/fhir/subscriptions.html

- **[S1429] W3C — WebSub Recommendation**  
  Why it matters: defines a subscription as a unique relation between a Topic URL and Subscriber Callback URL, allows lease-like expiration and renewal, and requires hubs to handle subscription requests and distribute updates, which anticipates amendment-recipient registries with endpoint and expiry discipline.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/websub/

- **[S1430] OpenID Foundation — Shared Signals Framework Specification 1.0**  
  Why it matters: formalizes transmitter/receiver event streams with stream IDs, audiences, delivery methods, endpoints, requested and delivered event types, subject inclusion and removal, stream lifecycle management, and inactivity handling, providing a template for managed amendment-recipient streams.  
  URL: https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html

- **[S1431] IETF RFC 8417 — Security Event Token (SET)**  
  Why it matters: defines signed or encryptable event tokens as issuer statements of fact about subjects that can be transmitted asynchronously, which supplies a useful envelope model for authenticated packet-amendment events.  
  URL: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8417

- **[S1432] IETF RFC 8935 — Push-Based Security Event Token Delivery Using HTTP**  
  Why it matters: defines delivery of SETs by HTTP POST over TLS to an intended recipient endpoint with success or failure indicated by the HTTP response, which models authenticated push delivery and failure evidence for amendment notices.  
  URL: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8935

- **[S1433] IETF RFC 8936 — Poll-Based Security Event Token Delivery Using HTTP**  
  Why it matters: defines poll-based retrieval and acknowledgement of event tokens, including assurance considerations, which supports amendment-recipient queues for parties that need controlled pull, batching, or receipt acknowledgement.  
  URL: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8936

- **[S1434] CNCF CloudEvents — Subscriptions API Specification**  
  Why it matters: defines mechanisms by which event consumers subscribe to events through a subscription manager that may determine who receives copies from producers, which maps closely onto brokers as event-routing intermediaries for corrected packets.  
  URL: https://github.com/cloudevents/spec/blob/main/subscriptions/spec.md

- **[S1435] HL7 FHIR — Consent Resource Definitions**  
  Why it matters: models grantors, grantees, managers, controllers, policy basis, actors, actions, purposes, resources, and effective periods, showing how recipient rights can be purpose-bound, actor-bound, time-bound, and policy-backed rather than merely address-bound.  
  URL: https://fhir.hl7.org/fhir/consent-definitions.html

- **[S1436] OASIS — eXtensible Access Control Markup Language (XACML) 3.0 Core Specification**  
  Why it matters: frames authorization decisions around attributes of subjects, resources, actions, and environment, providing a generic access-rights grammar for deciding which recipient may receive, rely on, forward, or challenge a packet amendment.  
  URL: https://docs.oasis-open.org/xacml/3.0/xacml-3.0-core-spec-os-en.html

- **[S1437] U.S. Courts — Federal Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 5**  
  Why it matters: distinguishes service on attorneys and parties, electronic service through court transmission facilities or consented means, address/change responsibilities, and ineffective service after a sender learns delivery failed, which gives a legal-notice analogy for recipient capacity, channel, consent, and failure knowledge.  
  URL: https://www.uscourts.gov/sites/default/files/2025-02/federal-rules-of-civil-procedure-dec-1-2024_0.pdf

- **[S1438] U.S. Code — 15 U.S.C. § 7001, Electronic Signatures in Global and National Commerce Act**  
  Why it matters: preserves underlying rights and obligations, says people generally cannot be forced to accept electronic records, and imposes consent conditions for electronic consumer disclosures, reinforcing that delivery channel, consent, and substantive rights remain distinct.  
  URL: https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?edition=prelim&num=0&req=granuleid%3AUSC-prelim-title15-section7001

- **[S1439] NIST OSCAL — Assessment Results Model Responsible-Party Structures**  
  Why it matters: defines responsible-party and responsible-role structures that create relationship arcs between role IDs and party UUIDs, supporting amendment-recipient registries as role-and-party graphs rather than flat contact lists.  
  URL: https://pages.nist.gov/OSCAL-Reference/models/v1.1.2/assessment-results/json-definitions/

## Identity-match appeals / broker support queues

- **[S1440] ONC — Patient Identity and Patient Record Matching**  
  Why it matters: defines patient matching as identifying and linking one patient’s data within and across health systems to obtain a comprehensive record, often by combining demographic fields, which establishes identity matching as critical interoperability infrastructure rather than clerical cleanup.  
  URL: https://healthit.gov/standards-and-technology/patient-identity-and-patient-record-matching/

- **[S1441] HL7 FHIR — Patient `$match` Operation**  
  Why it matters: describes a master-patient-index-style operation that accepts partial patient data and applies an algorithm to return likely matches, while noting that algorithm inputs and inactive merged-patient handling vary, which supports appeal paths for algorithmic match decisions.  
  URL: https://build.fhir.org/patient-operation-match.html

- **[S1442] HL7 FHIR — Interoperable Digital Identity and Patient Matching Guide**  
  Why it matters: profiles patient `$match` for cross-organizational use by authorized trusted parties, showing how identity matching becomes a multi-party infrastructure service with constrained participants and trust assumptions.  
  URL: https://build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/fhir-identity-matching-ig/patient-matching.html

- **[S1443] HL7 FHIR — Patient Resource, Merging and Linking Records**  
  Why it matters: distinguishes merging and linking duplicate patient records, notes that registration errors are a known problem, and describes accumulated downstream data, which provides a concrete model for split/merge correction and appeal effects.  
  URL: https://build.fhir.org/patient.html

- **[S1444] HL7 FHIR — Linkage Resource**  
  Why it matters: defines a resource for asserting linkages between multiple resource instances that refer to the same underlying business object, directly supporting the idea that same-subject assertions are explicit, reviewable objects.  
  URL: https://fhir.hl7.org/fhir/linkage.html

- **[S1445] IHE — Patient Identifier Cross-referencing for Mobile (PIXm), Volume 1**  
  Why it matters: assigns a Patient Identifier Cross-reference Manager the role of cross-referencing identities from different domains, leaves matching rules outside the profile, and notes manual linking or unlinking when rules and algorithms go wrong, which maps closely to appeal queues for brokered identity joins.  
  URL: https://build.fhir.org/ig/IHE/ITI.PIXm/volume-1.html

- **[S1446] IHE — Mobile Patient Identifier Cross-reference Query [ITI-83]**  
  Why it matters: defines a query that returns zero or more corresponding patient identifiers and specifies not-found and domain-not-recognized failure modes, giving a useful grammar for match results and appealable cross-reference errors.  
  URL: https://profiles.ihe.net/ITI/PIXm/ITI-83.html

- **[S1447] CVE Program — Policy and Procedure for Disputing a CVE Record**  
  Why it matters: formalizes a dispute path that begins with the responsible CNA or CNA-LR, escalates through Root and Top-Level Root structures, and can involve the Council of Roots for cross-hierarchy issues, providing a model for authoritative match and scope adjudication.  
  URL: https://www.cve.org/Resources/General/Policies/CVE-Record-Dispute-Policy.pdf

- **[S1448] CFPB — How Do I Dispute an Error on My Credit Report?**  
  Why it matters: describes an evidence-based dispute flow involving both the credit reporting company and the furnisher, including identifying the error, providing supporting documents, investigation, forwarding of evidence, results reporting, and statements of dispute, which is a mature model for packet redress queues.  
  URL: https://www.consumerfinance.gov/ask-cfpb/how-do-i-dispute-an-error-on-my-credit-report-en-314/

- **[S1449] CFPB Regulation V — 12 CFR § 1022.43 Direct Disputes**  
  Why it matters: requires furnishers to conduct reasonable investigations of covered direct disputes, review relevant information, report results, notify agencies of corrections, and explain frivolous or irrelevant determinations, supporting structured intake and outcome rules for broker appeals.  
  URL: https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1022/43/

- **[S1450] EUR-Lex — Regulation (EU) 2016/679, GDPR Articles 16, 18, and 19**  
  Why it matters: gives rights to rectification, restriction of processing while accuracy is contested, and notification of rectification, erasure, or restriction to recipients, which supports interim disputed states and downstream amendment-recipient duties after successful appeals.  
  URL: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/HTML/?from=SL&uri=CELEX%3A32016R0679

- **[S1451] GitHub Docs — Manage Dependabot Alerts**  
  Why it matters: describes auto-dismissed alerts, reopening dismissed alerts, and delegated alert dismissal, demonstrating native security workflows where automated alert state can be revisited by authorized actors.  
  URL: https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/manage-security-alerts/manage-dependabot-alerts

- **[S1452] Atlassian Jira — Configuring Issue Linking**  
  Why it matters: lists default issue-link relation types such as relates to, duplicates / is duplicated by, blocks / is blocked by, and clones / is cloned by, showing the local relation vocabularies that brokers must normalize and later adjudicate.  
  URL: https://confluence.atlassian.com/adminjiraserver101/configuring-issue-linking-1455429134.html

- **[S1453] GitLab Docs — Linked Issues and Issue Management**  
  Why it matters: describes bidirectional linked issues, visibility depending on permissions, relation categories, and issue actions such as clone and mark-as-duplicate, which supplies another native relation grammar for cross-tool identity-match appeals.  
  URL: https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/issues/related_issues/

## Correction-materiality thresholds / packet boilerplate

- **[S1454] SEC Staff Accounting Bulletin No. 99 — Materiality**  
  Why it matters: rejects exclusive reliance on quantitative benchmarks and emphasizes qualitative materiality considerations, supporting packet schedules where small numeric corrections can still trigger reliance, eligibility, integrity, or non-reliance consequences.  
  URL: https://www.sec.gov/interps/account/sab99.htm

- **[S1455] SEC Staff Accounting Bulletin No. 108 — Quantifying Financial Statement Misstatements**  
  Why it matters: addresses how prior-period misstatements carry over or reverse when quantifying current misstatements, which supports cumulative correction thresholds for renewal counts, residue age, denominator drift, and repeated immaterial packet errors.  
  URL: https://www.sec.gov/rules-regulations/staff-guidance/staff-accounting-bulletins/staff-accounting-bulletin-no-108

- **[S1456] SEC Form 8-K — Item 4.02 Non-Reliance on Previously Issued Financial Statements or Related Audit Reports**  
  Why it matters: provides a public-reporting model for when an error crosses into a formal non-reliance event, which maps onto packet states where earlier versions become unsafe for specified reliance purposes.  
  URL: https://www.sec.gov/files/form8-k.pdf

- **[S1457] PCAOB Auditing Standard AS 2810 — Evaluating Audit Results**  
  Why it matters: requires auditors to evaluate material misstatements, qualitative aspects, intentionality, bias, and the sufficiency of evidence, providing a template for materiality labels that cannot be reduced to score deltas alone.  
  URL: https://pcaobus.org/oversight/standards/auditing-standards/details/AS2810

- **[S1458] Federal Rules of Civil Procedure — Rule 26(e), Supplementing Disclosures and Responses**  
  Why it matters: imposes a duty to supplement or correct incomplete or incorrect disclosures in material respects, offering a legal analogue for packet corrections that must move from internal repair to disclosed amendment.  
  URL: https://www.law.cornell.edu/rules/frcp/rule_26

- **[S1459] Federal Rules of Civil Procedure — Rule 37(c), Failure to Disclose or Supplement**  
  Why it matters: distinguishes sanctionable nondisclosure from failures that are substantially justified or harmless, supporting packet boilerplate that separates material correction failures from harmless ones.  
  URL: https://www.law.cornell.edu/rules/frcp/rule_37

- **[S1460] GDPR Article 33 — Notification of a Personal Data Breach to the Supervisory Authority**  
  Why it matters: sets a risk-tiered notification threshold and requires documentation of facts, effects, and remedial action, giving a pattern for recordkeeping below the notice line and escalation above it.  
  URL: https://gdpr-info.eu/art-33-gdpr/

- **[S1461] GDPR Article 34 — Communication of a Personal Data Breach to the Data Subject**  
  Why it matters: distinguishes supervisory notification from direct communication to affected people when high risk is likely, supporting recipient-class-specific materiality thresholds rather than a single all-purpose correction rule.  
  URL: https://gdpr-info.eu/art-34-gdpr/

- **[S1462] CFPB Regulation V — 12 CFR § 1022.43 Direct Disputes**  
  Why it matters: requires reasonable investigation of covered direct disputes, correction notifications, and reasoned frivolous-or-irrelevant determinations, supporting appeal outcome categories for corrected, verified, unverifiable, irrelevant, insufficient, and corrected-but-immaterial packet disputes.  
  URL: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1022/subpart-E/section-1022.43

- **[S1463] NIST National Vulnerability Database — Vulnerability Metrics / CVSS**  
  Why it matters: describes CVSS as a qualitative severity measure rather than a complete risk measure and explains 0-to-10 scoring, which supports separate packet thresholds for score movement, severity-band crossing, and risk-policy effect.  
  URL: https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln-metrics/cvss

- **[S1464] FIRST — CVSS v4.0 Specification Document**  
  Why it matters: defines CVSS metric groups and qualitative severity structures, giving a mature example of why normalized packet corrections may need band-crossing and vector-change treatment in addition to numeric deltas.  
  URL: https://www.first.org/cvss/v4.0/specification-document

- **[S1465] FedRAMP Documentation — Significant Change Notifications**  
  Why it matters: establishes conditions under which significant changes may proceed without advance approval while preserving agency visibility and certain escalation conditions, providing a model for tiered correction classes and approval thresholds.  
  URL: https://www.fedramp.gov/docs/rev5/balance/significant-change-notifications/

- **[S1466] eCFR — 21 CFR Part 806, Medical Device Corrections and Removals Reports and Records**  
  Why it matters: separates reportable corrections and removals initiated to reduce health risk or remedy risky violations from non-reportable corrections that still require records, supporting packet schedules that maintain records below the notice threshold.  
  URL: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-21/chapter-I/subchapter-H/part-806/subpart-B

## Appeal-stay labels / reliance controls

- **[S1467] ICO — Right to Restrict Processing**  
  Why it matters: explains restriction as an interim state in which data can often be stored but most other processing is limited while accuracy or another condition is contested, giving a direct analogue for appealed packet subjects that remain present but not fully usable.  
  URL: https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/individual-rights/individual-rights/right-to-restrict-processing/

- **[S1468] European Data Protection Board — Respect Individuals’ Rights: Restriction of Processing**  
  Why it matters: describes restriction of processing when data accuracy is disputed and notes downstream recipient notification, supporting packet stay labels that both constrain use and propagate to prior recipients.  
  URL: https://www.edpb.europa.eu/sme-data-protection-guide/respect-individuals-rights_en

- **[S1469] U.S. Code — 15 U.S.C. § 1681i, Procedure in Case of Disputed Accuracy**  
  Why it matters: requires reinvestigation of disputed credit information, recording current status or deleting unverified items, and permits statements of dispute when reinvestigation does not resolve the issue, offering a mature disputed-state model.  
  URL: https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?edition=prelim&num=0&req=granuleid%3AUSC-prelim-title15-section1681i

- **[S1470] eCFR — 12 CFR § 1022.43, Direct Disputes**  
  Why it matters: requires furnishers to investigate covered direct disputes, review relevant information, report results, and notify consumers of frivolous or irrelevant determinations, supporting appeal-stay evidence thresholds and anti-abuse filters.  
  URL: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1022/subpart-E/section-1022.43

- **[S1471] FTC — Consumer Reports: What Information Furnishers Need to Know**  
  Why it matters: states that once information is disputed, furnishers may not continue reporting it without indicating that it is disputed, supporting packet labels that travel with contested subjects rather than remaining inside an appeal inbox.  
  URL: https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/consumer-reports-what-information-furnishers-need-know

- **[S1472] FAR 33.104 — Protests to GAO**  
  Why it matters: describes GAO protest handling, award timing, performance suspension, and override concepts, providing a procurement analogue for appeals that can stay downstream action unless a named override path is used.  
  URL: https://www.acquisition.gov/far/33.104

- **[S1473] eCFR — 4 CFR § 21.6, Withholding of Award and Suspension of Contract Performance**  
  Why it matters: states that a GAO protest may require withholding award or suspending performance and that agencies must file notice when overriding such requirements, supporting formal stay labels and override notices.  
  URL: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-4/chapter-I/subchapter-B/part-21/section-21.6

- **[S1474] Federal Rules of Appellate Procedure — Rule 8, Stay or Injunction Pending Appeal**  
  Why it matters: provides the procedural model for a stay, injunction, modification, restoration, or suspension while an appeal is pending, supporting packet labels that distinguish appeal filing from interim relief.  
  URL: https://www.law.cornell.edu/rules/frap/rule_8

- **[S1475] Federal Rules of Civil Procedure — Rule 62, Stay of Proceedings to Enforce a Judgment**  
  Why it matters: creates an automatic stay period and allows stays by bond or other security, supporting packet concepts such as automatic stay windows, security-backed holdback reserves, and stay expiry.  
  URL: https://www.law.cornell.edu/rules/frcp/rule_62

- **[S1476] HL7 FHIR — Task Status CodeSystem**

  URL: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8693

- **[S1559] Model Context Protocol — Authorization specification**  
  Why it matters: anchors AI-tool authorization in OAuth 2.1-style mechanisms, protected-resource metadata, authorization-server discovery, secure token storage, short-lived tokens, refresh-token rotation, and PKCE.  
  URL: https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/authorization

- **[S1560] OpenID Foundation — Identity Management for Agentic AI announcement**  
  Why it matters: identifies agent identity, delegated authority, lifecycle management, governance, and accountability as emerging standards gaps for AI-agent systems.  
  URL: https://openid.net/new-whitepaper-tackles-ai-agent-identity-challenges/

- **[S1561] IETF Internet-Draft — Agent Authorization Profile for OAuth 2.0**  
  Why it matters: early standards-track work-in-progress describing structured claims for agent identity, task context, operational constraints, delegation chains, proof-of-possession, and human oversight.  
  URL: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-aap-oauth-profile/

- **[S1562] IRS — Form 2848 and online power-of-attorney submission**  
  Why it matters: shows a mature representative-of-record domain where authority grants permit eligible representatives to act and inspect confidential information, and where digital submission / Tax Pro Account workflows are becoming operational infrastructure.  
  URL: https://www.irs.gov/forms-pubs/about-form-2848

- **[S1563] GOV.UK — Manage a lasting power of attorney**  
  Why it matters: documents registered LPA use, attorney duties, joint/separate decision modes, restrictions, confidentiality, recordkeeping, and misuse constraints, supporting authority-state and proxy-abuse telemetry.  
  URL: https://www.gov.uk/manage-lasting-power-attorney

- **[S1564] IETF Internet-Draft — OAuth 2.0 Agents Native Authorization via Structured Elicitation**  
  Why it matters: records emerging work on just-in-time authorization for AI agents acting on behalf of users in human-to-agent flows.  
  URL: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-embesozzi-oauth-agent-native-authorization/


## rev0187 provenance-lineage sources

- **[S1565] W3C — PROV-DM: The PROV Data Model**  
  Why it matters: defines provenance through entities, activities, agents, usage, generation, derivation, association, attribution, and provenance of provenance; anchors the refactor's entity/activity/agent split.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-dm/

- **[S1566] W3C — PROV-O: The PROV Ontology**  
  Why it matters: supplies an ontology for agents, activities, entities, derivation, and responsibility relationships that can be reused across data, media, records, and proof objects.  
  URL: https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-o/

- **[S1567] C2PA — Content provenance and authenticity standard**  
  Why it matters: provides an open technical standard for content origin and edits, validating the media-provenance and edit-history branch of the lineage family.  
  URL: https://c2pa.org/

- **[S1568] OpenLineage — Object model**  
  Why it matters: models datasets, jobs, run events, design-time events, and lineage observations as data is created and transformed; supports the claim that transformation history becomes operational evidence.  
  URL: https://openlineage.io/docs/spec/object-model/

- **[S1569] OpenLineage — Facets and column-level lineage**  
  Why it matters: exposes extensible metadata and fine-grained column-level dependency patterns, supporting diffing, replay, and normalization-loss claims.  
  URL: https://openlineage.io/docs/spec/facets/

- **[S1570] SLSA — Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts**  
  Why it matters: treats artifact integrity, tamper resistance, and supply-chain visibility as cross-industry control surfaces, supporting custody and replay-bundle dossiers.  
  URL: https://slsa.dev/

- **[S1571] in-toto — Software supply-chain integrity framework**  
  Why it matters: makes the performed steps, actors, and order of a supply chain transparent to users, supporting stepwise custody, transformation, and attestable process evidence.  
  URL: https://in-toto.io/

- **[S1572] SPDX — Overview**  
  Why it matters: defines an open standard for communicating software bill-of-material information including provenance, license, security, and related metadata.  
  URL: https://spdx.dev/about/overview/

- **[S1573] SPDX 3.0.1 — AI profile**  
  Why it matters: extends the BOM/provenance grammar into AI systems, model artifacts, datasets, and packages, supporting lineage obligations around derived AI data and models.  
  URL: https://spdx.github.io/spdx-spec/v3.0.1/model/AI/AI/

- **[S1574] GS1 Digital Link — URI syntax and resolver standards**  
  Why it matters: separates product identifiers, Web URI syntax, resolver behavior, linksets, validation, default links, and granular key qualifiers; validates resolver-successor and passport-continuity concerns.  
  URL: https://ref.gs1.org/standards/digital-link/

- **[S1575] GS1-Conformant Resolver Standard**  
  Why it matters: specifies resolver operation, link redirection, linksets, validation, fallback/error codes, and description files, grounding resolver-governance and resolver-continuity claims.  
  URL: https://ref.gs1.org/standards/resolver/

- **[S1576] European Commission — Digital Product Passport consultation**  
  Why it matters: asks how passport data should be stored and managed by service providers and whether a service-provider certification scheme is needed, supporting custody, resolver, and service-provider continuity theses.  
  URL: https://single-market-economy.ec.europa.eu/news/commission-launches-consultation-digital-product-passport-2025-04-09_en

## rev0188 exposure-liability sources

- **[S1577] European Commission — Liability for defective products / new Product Liability Directive**  
  Why it matters: official summary of the new PLD entering into force in December 2024, covering digital products, software and AI systems, update-related defects, 10/25-year liability tails, disclosure of evidence, and the 9 December 2026 application/transposition milestone.  
  URL: https://single-market-economy.ec.europa.eu/single-market/goods/free-movement-sectors/liability-defective-products_en

- **[S1578] SEC — Public Company Cybersecurity Disclosures; Final Rules fact sheet**  
  Why it matters: requires material cybersecurity incident disclosure on Form 8-K generally within four business days after materiality determination, and annual disclosure of cyber risk-management, strategy, and governance.  
  URL: https://www.sec.gov/files/33-11216-fact-sheet.pdf

- **[S1579] SEC Division of Corporation Finance — Disclosure of Cybersecurity Incidents Determined To Be Material and Other Cybersecurity Incidents**  
  Why it matters: clarifies that Item 1.05 is for incidents determined material, while voluntary or pre-materiality disclosures should be routed differently to avoid diluting material-incident signals.  
  URL: https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/speeches-statements/gerding-cybersecurity-incidents-05212024

- **[S1580] Lloyd's Market Bulletin Y5381 — State-backed cyber-attack exclusions**  
  Why it matters: requires suitable state-backed cyber-attack exclusions in standalone cyber-attack policies from 31 March 2023, including robust attribution basis and clear key terms.  
  URL: https://assets.lloyds.com/media/35926dc8-c885-497b-aed8-6d2f87c1415d/Y5381%20Market%20Bulletin%20-%20Cyber-attack%20exclusions.pdf

- **[S1581] Lloyd's Market Bulletin Y5433 — State-backed cyber-attack wordings**  
  Why it matters: frames state-backed cyber coverage as something that, where underwritten, must be controlled and measurable because such attacks can create systemic-loss exposure.  
  URL: https://assets.lloyds.com/media/6335bcb0-e2a2-4378-8328-1ddf54828f2f/Y5433.pdf

- **[S1582] NAIC — 2025 Report on the Cybersecurity Insurance Market**  
  Why it matters: official insurance-regulator report on U.S. and global cyber insurance using annual statement cybersecurity insurance supplement and surplus-lines data, grounding the underwriting / loss-run / protection-gap layer.  
  URL: https://content.naic.org/sites/default/files/inline-files/2025_Cybersecurity_Insurance%20Report.pdf

- **[S1583] Federal Register / CISA — Cyber Incident Reporting for Critical Infrastructure Act reporting requirements NPRM**  
  Why it matters: proposes covered cyber-incident reporting within 72 hours of reasonable belief, ransom-payment reporting within 24 hours, supplemental reports, and substantially-similar-reporting exceptions.  
  URL: https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2024/04/04/2024-06526/cyber-incident-reporting-for-critical-infrastructure-act-circia-reporting-requirements

- **[S1584] EIOPA — Cyber insurance**  
  Why it matters: frames cyber risk for insurers as both operational risk and underwriting risk, and links insurance, resilience, SupTech, and cyber-underwriting questions.  
  URL: https://www.eiopa.europa.eu/browse/digitalisation-and-financial-innovation/cyber-insurance_en

- **[S1585] EIOPA — Methodological principles of insurance stress testing, cyber component**  
  Why it matters: treats cyber resilience as the ability of insurers to sustain operational and financial effects of cyber events, with stress metrics such as outage time, business processes affected, recovery times, and sensitive-data breached.  
  URL: https://www.eiopa.europa.eu/publications/methodological-principles-insurance-stress-testing-cyber-component_en
